**Images and devices used**: the fifteenth entry in the notebook of dreams (Zakharych's white birds over the shed
that is the foundry; nobody rang); the May Day postcard stood against the kettle, later laid face down, at the end
"back in the sideboard"; Zakharych's plywood and the scarred cock; "Second-hand"; the house with half a zoo; "like
an eagle on his prey"; the handful of fluff; "Bambi"; the neck an inch from the fangs; the sheepdog "stiff as a
stool" and the leg on the fence; the cage and the sky; "hunts alone by spring"; the Mouse and the Dove; people in
fur and feathers; the collars; two crowns and a bridge; claws cut on Saturdays; the axe by the birch logs; the
stone picked up in an afternoon; the queue for boots; "You are Gaika. — Thank you"; the glove held by the cold one;
the jeep and the trailer; "after you"; the forum; the plate and the grey coat; the town as a map; the white rag on
a stick; the drum and the uniform; the fuse in every head; two officers with two keys; the poplars pulled up
"without malice"; the homing missile; the wasps' nest and the lecture; the brake written by dead wolves; "who can
unscrew it"; the turkey and the peacock; the window for complaints; the teaser (the fine from a camera on the
bypass, fifty-eight where the sign says forty, "Your appeal has been considered" in four minutes, the firm and the
fund, "Whose face do I punch? — That is the invention... An old one. Older than the cannon").

**Facts used**. **Checked on the day of writing against the texts**: Lorenz, *King Solomon's Ring* (German original
1949, English 1952), chapter 12, "Morals and Weapons", read in full in an open scan (the doves: "a tame,
home-reared male turtle-dove and a female ring-dove", the African blond ring-dove, a roomy cage, Vienna, "the next
day", "like an eagle on his prey", "Without my interference she would undoubtedly have finished him off"; the two
other instances, cichlid fishes and "as a field surgeon, in the late war"; the hares; the roe buck and Hornaday's
statistics, not used; the wolves of Whipsnade and "he would like to, but he just cannot"; the leg; the raven Roah,
not used beyond the row of the figure; why the ring-dove needs no inhibition; the turkey and the peacock; Homer and
the knight; man as the one being whose weapons do not grow on his body; the closing question, first printed in
November 1935); Maynard Smith & Price, "The Logic of Animal Conflict" (*Nature* 246: 15–18, 1973,
doi:10.1038/246015a0: the strategies Mouse, Hawk, Bully, Retaliator and Prober-Retaliator, two thousand contests of
each of fifteen types, Retaliator as the stable strategy, the explanation by "group selection" set aside; the word
"Dove" does not occur in the paper); Gómez, Verdú, González-Megías & Méndez, "The phylogenetic roots of human
lethal violence" (*Nature* 538: 233–237, 2016, doi:10.1038/nature19758: 1,024 mammal species, more than four
million deaths, 0.30 ± 0.19% of deaths caused by conspecifics, lethal violence reported for almost 40% of the
species, 2.0 ± 0.02% inferred for humans and similar for the ancestor of primates and apes; state societies below
the inference; the figures for single species such as the meerkat are in the supplement and were not read);
Wrangham, "Two types of aggression in human evolution" (*PNAS* 115: 245–253, online December 2017,
doi:10.1073/pnas.1713611115: reactive and proactive aggression; "two to three orders of magnitude less than in
chimpanzees and bonobos" for the one human population with measured rates of fighting, Australian aborigines "in
difficult circumstances"; Boehm's "execution hypothesis" and the signs of self-domestication); Nazaretyan's own
English wording of the law ("the higher up a society's technologies, both producing and military, the more refined
the regulatory means required for its self-preservation") and of the Bloodshed Ratio with its "irregular downward
trend" (an abstract of his; the paper "Power and Wisdom", *Journal for the Theory of Social Behaviour*, 2003,
doi:10.1046/j.1468-5914.2003.00224.x, is linked and was not read); the homicide series for England and Wales on Our
World in Data, from Eisner 2014 (21.4 per 100,000 a year for 1300–1349, 0.8 around 1912–1962). **Checked against
abstracts, summaries and records**: Milgram's proximity series (65%, 62.5%, 40% and 30% of 40 subjects; "Behavioral
Study of Obedience", 1963, and "Some Conditions of Obedience and Disobedience to Authority", *Human Relations* 18:
57–76, 1965, doi:10.1177/001872676501800105; the papers themselves were not read); Cassidy and others in
*Yellowstone Science* 24(1), 2016 (intraspecific strife as two thirds of the natural mortality of collared wolves,
1995–2015); Lapidot-Lefler & Barak, "Effects of anonymity, invisibility, and lack of eye-contact on toxic online
disinhibition" (*Computers in Human Behavior* 28: 434–443, 2012, doi:10.1016/j.chb.2011.10.014: 142 participants,
lack of eye contact "the chief contributor"; the abstract only); Pinker's "tenfold-to-fiftyfold decline" in
European homicide (*The Better Angels of Our Nature*, 2011, quoted from the book's Wikipedia page; the book is
unnamed in the text, "a thick book"); the English Wikipedia page on Picasso's *Dove* (the lithograph of 9 January
1949, the Milanese pigeon given by Matisse, chosen by Aragon for the poster of the Paris congress of April 1949)
and a page of the Picasso administration that gives, after Geneviève Laporte, his remark that no animal is as
cruel as a pigeon (second-hand, and said so in the text); the Russian Wikipedia pages for Picasso's dove, Lorenz
and Nazaretyan (through the API; the Russian page "Кольцо царя Соломона" is a disambiguation page, so the Russian
text links to Lorenz). **From memory, not checked**: that Picasso kept pigeons from boyhood (his father painted
them); the grey coat of Milgram's experimenter; the white flag as an old custom; fraternisation between the
trenches (the Christmas truce of 1914, unnamed); two officers with two keys at a missile launch; a bomber crew of
about ten; Bambi as a roe deer in the Austrian novel of 1923. The author's sources: the forum posts of 7 November
2013 (the ants and the poplars; "дело не в агрессивности ИИ, а в агрессивности людей... самонаводящаяся на цель
ракета"); the comments under 14 and under the seminar post of 2012 on Nazaretyan (the two angles that complement
each other; he "присутствовал на моем докладе"; the mechanisms that restrained aggression "работать перестанут");
article 14 (the ever more perfect weapon; "what can happen will happen"); the mark of 30. Invented for the frame
(Oleg's side): the dream, the postcard, Zakharych and his loft, the neighbour's sheepdog, the queue for boots in
1990, the jeep on the bypass, the wasps' nest, the fine and the four minutes. Not used: Petrov in 1983 and
Arkhipov in 1962 (a man who did not press; a candidate for 59 or 65); Marshall's figures on soldiers who did not
fire (disputed); the Hague declaration of 1899 on projectiles from balloons and the ban on the crossbow of 1139;
the meerkat as the most murderous mammal (not verified); Schenkel's correction of Lorenz's reading of the offered
neck (1967); tears as a signal (the heap "not sorted" of 46 is left as it was); Lorenz's *On Aggression* (1963)
and its "hydraulic" drive; the baby schema; replications and critiques of Milgram by name; bonobos; current wars,
weapons that choose their targets and anything of today's politics (the plan forbids them).

**Figures**: `brakes-by-the-fangs` (the table in the ash: the dove and the hare, the wolf and the raven, man; the
third row is Andrey's reading), `the-face-moves-away` (Milgram's four runs, counts of 40), `what-held-the-blow`
(the three supports; a reading, the third support is the continuation's).

**Left for later**: who the complaint goes to, and the person without a face → 55 (Oleg's last doubt: the fine from
a camera, "Whose face do I punch? — That is the invention... Older than the cannon"; the answer to "who owns the
machines" of 34 and 49 is still in the notebook); whether a brake taught into a machine holds against selection by
buyers ("who can unscrew it") → open, a candidate for a dated mark at the check of 2028 and for the row "relation
to us" of 64; the mark of 30 on the war of machines → 67; what a man who understands this does (the author's
"refuse to return aggression, withdraw") and the happy little screw → 65; mass aggression as an instrument of
apoptosis (14) was touched in one line and not developed: 54 gives the mechanism (the supports go), not a
forecast; the heap "not sorted" of 46 (tears, shame) is still not sorted.

## 55. A Person Without a Face

**Established**
- The answer to the teaser of 54 ("Whose face do I punch? — That is the invention... Older than the cannon"). Oleg
  comes with the envelope of the fine. **The fine is made exact, and 54 is not contradicted**: the letter says
  "Speed recorded: fifty-eight. Speed permitted: twenty"; the sign says forty (he drove out to look); in April the
  road menders stood a temporary "twenty" on a yellow board by the bridge and took it away in May, "and in the
  camera's table it is still April". By the sign he was eighteen over, "and for less than twenty over nobody is
  fined at all"; by the table thirty-eight over: 750 roubles, or 562.50 "with a quarter off... A discount for
  keeping quiet". The form answered in four minutes: "No grounds for cancelling the decision have been found."
- **Three days looking for a face**: the telephone ("By 'press seven' I understood that she was not a woman"); the
  turkey of 54 ("a pensioner, hauling firewood. It pecks"); room 312 of a business centre by the station, locked,
  "some forty firms are registered in that room", the post collected on Thursdays; the register in the boy's phone
  (the director appointed in March, the owner a fund, "who is behind the fund is not written there"); the road
  menders are another firm. **"Everybody is right."**
- The epigraph is answered with the saying credited to an English judge (Thurlow, unnamed): "they have neither
  bodies to be punished nor souls to be condemned, and so they do as they like."
- **The descent**: who fined you? The camera ("a piece of iron on a pole"), the firm ("Show it to me. — Room three
  hundred and twelve"), the director, the owner: the fine stays whichever of them goes. Oleg's plant: in 1996 it
  owed him three months' wages; not the director ("five directors in those years"), not the walls, not the people
  ("They were the ones it owed"); the owner changed too (the state, a joint-stock company, the holding of 42),
  "and the debt to me was one and the same debt". The work-record book of 1982: "The plant. A round seal."
  **"All your working life you worked for somebody who is not a man, not a wall and not a lathe."**
- **The word**: "A legal person... I never once thought about the second word." Latin *persona*, the mask of an
  actor: "The lawyers took the word for a mask and did not hide it. — A mask with nobody under it. — A person
  without a face." (In the Russian text the pun is native: «юридическое лицо», «лицо без лица».)
- **Why it was made: the column "who" of 50.** "A court has the same form as a sentence: who owes, who owns, who
  answers. It is not accepted empty: debts cannot be kept without names" (43). A town: the mayor dies, the townsmen
  change (Oleg quotes 40: "What remains when the people change... And the form has no line for that"). "So a name
  was written in the column, and everybody agreed to count the name as somebody. — The deputy for general
  questions. — With a seal and an account."
- **The history, in the order told**: the 1240s, "a church lawyer, the best of his day" (Innocent IV, unnamed; no
  religion argued): he forbade punishing a town or a college as a whole, only the guilty in it, and in his
  commentary the college "is imagined as one person". "Everything that takes a pocket, yes. Everything that takes a
  body, no." The English judge of 1612 (Coke): "invisible, immortal"; it cannot be outlawed, "for it has no soul".
  **The dead hand**: the statute of 1279 ("The hand that does not die was called dead. — Lawyers"); Oleg: "Heaps
  with neither heirs nor funerals (49). And I thought we had made them up over the matches. — They were noticed in
  1279... It held badly." The favour of kings: the charter of the last day of 1600 for two hundred-odd London
  merchants, fifteen years of trade beyond the Cape; two hundred years later "that paper kept an army" (**the
  plan's "a quarter of a million soldiers in 1800" is made careful**: about two hundred thousand at the start of the
  nineteenth century and a quarter of a million a little later, twice the crown's; taxes from tens of millions; run
  from one house in a London street; thirty-five permanent clerks in the head office a hundred years into its
  history). "Whose was the army? — The shareholders'. — Which of them gave the orders? — None... — Then who ruled
  India? — Fill in the column. — The Company." Then "the favour became a service": registration in 1844, limited
  liability in 1855 ("I took it for a warning. — It is one"). **"That is all a person in law is: a name and a
  pocket"** (the pocket of 47); "The arithmetic came first, and the law was written after it" (24).
- **Today**: "Three working days and ten thousand roubles of capital. File it through the phone, and there is not
  even a fee. — And a man? — Nine months. And twenty years of school (53)." The American state with about a
  million people and more than two million such persons, and the "one low house" that was the address of 285,000 of
  them in 2012 ("Room three hundred and twelve. — A large one"). Who can be one: a town, a monastery, a
  university, a firm, a fund, a ship, and **the plan's line to retell, the river** (the Whanganui, 2017: "a legal
  person, with all the rights, powers, duties and liabilities of one"; two people speak for it, and the act calls
  the pair "the human face" of the river: "Two deputies for general questions").
- **"Who owns the tractor" (the line of 34, still in the notebook) is answered and crossed out.** Oleg: "there is
  always somebody under a mask, or it is not a mask, it is a scarecrow." The chain: the camera is the firm's, the
  firm the fund's, "that is where the register stops". The count of 2017: in nearly nine of ten of the five hundred
  largest American companies the largest holder is "three funds, if you count them together" ("Three rich men. —
  Three persons without a face"); about a fifth of nearly everything and about a quarter of the votes; "they mostly
  do not choose what to buy... each firm in proportion to its size" ("To him who has" of 49. "— By a table").
  Whose money: tens of millions of savers; the granddaughter's "fund on everything", two thousand a month: "She
  cannot even find out which ones they are... A little is added every year. — The feed. And the say?" **"The feed
  comes down the chain. The say does not go up it"** ("A pet of the pension fund (52), and she is not thirty yet").
  **"Ownership protects the owner" of 34 is narrowed**: "It does, while the owner holds the wheel. A farmer in 1935
  owned his tractor: he sat on it." The author's line of June 2017: the owner determines only the fact that the
  plant exists; what it does is determined by everybody it deals with ("That was against the Marxists. — It was. It
  fits here as well"). And "people" are not all of us: the richest tenth of American households holds nearly nine
  tenths of all the shares, the poorer half less than one share in a hundred.
- **Oleg's objection (the plan's), in three parts, "and every one is true".** (1) *Behind every firm, an owner*:
  the Nobel money (the owner died in 1896; a fund of 1900 with "no owners at all: no shares, no heirs. It has a
  paper written by a dead man, and a board that may not depart from the paper"); the furniture shops that belong to
  a fund set up by the founder in 1982, which by its own papers has no owners ("The founder died in 2018, and the
  shops did not notice"); the profit "stays inside" (the recipient of 37). "So the owner can be a paper. — A paper
  written by a dead man. That... is the other thing lawyers call the dead hand." (2) *The director answers in
  person*: "It is the place where the law still asks for a face"; the camera firm's director is listed in eleven
  more firms: **"Funt. The chairman for sitting"** (the 1931 novel, quoted); "A face for hire. A trade, and an old
  one: the law asks for a signature, and it gets one" (the woman on a Caribbean island listed as running more than
  twelve hundred companies, 2012). The shield of the signature of 24: "He signs. Does he decide? — He doesn't know
  where the camera stands." **A refinement of 52's "right of signature"**: "There is a quieter way: the signature
  stays, and the hand that makes it decides nothing." The law does not ask for a face everywhere: in Russia a
  director's powers may be handed to a managing organisation; the American state's law of 2021 ("managed by a
  program"), softened a year later, "And a member it must have. At least one." "A heap lives where it pays less
  (49). And a person in law is born where it is asked the fewest questions." (3) *The state closes them*: in 1772
  the Company could not pay "and the state did not close it. The state lent it money: too much hung on it" (Oleg:
  "The cord", 51); in 1858 the crown took India: "The clerks were the same, the army the same, the taxes the same.
  The name on the door was different"; struck off in 1874, "and hardly anybody noticed"; the oil firm cut in 1911
  into thirty-odd pieces that together were worth more than the whole. **Conceded in full**: "A person in law lives
  by a line in a register, and whoever keeps the register can strike the line out. That is more power than anybody
  has over a man." But who used it: "Another person without a face, and the oldest of them all: the crown, the
  treasury" ("The state is people. — Which ones? They change, and the treaties and the debts stay. Fill in the
  column"), and when: "when it could do the job itself. The rule of the switch" (51). "A law is a want written
  down (24)... It is written by those who are needed, for as long as they are needed" (25).
- **"Then let us write one. There shall be a window, and a face in it."** It has been written (Europe, 2018: the
  right not to be judged by a program alone). "And what does the law get? Often enough, a man past whom the
  program's decisions run, with a button 'agree' under each" (the keepers of that law had to write that a man put
  there for show does not count). "Funt again." **"The law can ask for a face. What it is given is a signature."**
- **The symmetry test**: Oleg: "For thirty suppliers the plant was me... We are the ones who hold the tool. — What
  was the name of the office that took you on? — The personnel department... Later they renamed it. 'Human
  resources.' — The spade had its say."
- **The machines are brought in by Oleg, after the middle.** "How many people are there in the firm that fined
  you? — 'Average number of employees: four'... And its cameras stand over half the region." The work is counted:
  the camera saw, a program read the plate, a machine printed and sealed ("Nobody licks forty thousand envelopes"),
  "Who took the complaint? Who answered it in four minutes? — One of those. I'll say it, since you are sitting with
  your mouth shut again." The four: "The one who sits. A bookkeeper. And two who climb the poles." **The lock with
  54**: "the hands are leaving the chain. This is where the chain is kept. A person in law was always made of
  people, and those people were its face." The captain of twenty years ago ("go home, father. Two minutes"); "He
  might have said: come on Thursday... Or dropped a hint. This one drops no hints... It is the same for everybody.
  — I am not saying it is worse. I am saying what it is. The captain was a face inside a person that has none: a
  head with a brake in it, and eyes that could go and look at the sign." **"Which of the replacements needed a new
  law? — None. The firm bought a program the way we used to buy typewriters... — The shell is the same. The filling
  is different."**
- **Nobody will grant rights to machines.** Oleg: "A program is a thing... it would have to be given rights. A
  passport. And who will vote for that? I shan't." The European Parliament's request of 2017 ("electronic person"),
  the letter of some hundreds of specialists, "the idea went no further"; several American states have written the
  opposite into law. "They were arguing whether to sew a new suit, and decided not to." What one of those needs: "To
  own. To sign a contract. To pay and to be paid. To go to court. — Four things. And who has had all four for four
  hundred years?" **"The suit is hanging in the wardrobe. Three days and ten thousand roubles. Nobody has to grant
  a machine any rights. It is enough to put it inside something that has them."** Whether there is anybody inside
  to be pitied is "another question" (20), "and the law will come to it late. To own, nobody's pity is needed."
- **The wallet with no person behind it (the thing to watch of 47) gets its portrait and stays a thing to watch**:
  "It will not look like a robot with a purse. It will look like a line in a register: a name, an account, somebody
  who sits. And inside, copies." The lawyer's argument of 2014 (a firm left with no members, the charter says "do
  what the program decides"); "Lawyers dispute whether a court would let it stand, and no court has been asked. I
  know of no clean case: wherever a program 'runs a firm' today, there is a man behind it, and the account is in
  his name"; the draft of the summer of 2026 in "the state with the low house" (a firm whose daily affairs are
  managed by a program, with one member who answers for it): "A draft... Only note what would be new in it. Not
  the shell." **No mark and no date** (the plan gives none for 55).
- **The author's forecast of January 2017, quoted as his**: systems built to keep working, return the investment to
  the formal owners, keep supplied, widen the paying market, defend against freeloaders ("That is the charter of
  any firm. — It is"); if the owners disappear, one task falls away, the rest stay, "and anybody who tries to
  reprogram the system it will take for a hacker". Oleg: "And me? — You come under the fifth point... — A
  freeloader. At sixty-two." **The continuation's reading**: "It read like a film script then. And I was only
  describing the fund with the prize... With one difference. The fund's board is made of people, and any one of
  them can still say: this is not what he meant."
- **Oleg uses the test of 50 on Andrey again** ("'The firm wants.' Who? The long way"). The long way: nobody at the
  plant wanted the plan, and the plan was met ("Mostly"), "because the plant was put together that way"; the firms
  put together otherwise "are not there to be looked at" (half of all firms gone in ten years, 40): "What is left
  looks as if it wanted." And the charter: in 1600 the paper named the job, today's says "Any activity not
  forbidden by law"; what is left when the job is taken out: "To keep going. To have the means. To bring in more
  than it spends. — Wants that nobody set" (22). The writer of 2017 (Stross, unnamed): artificial minds beside us
  for centuries, "very old and very slow". Oleg: "I sat in the meetings they think with. — Now the meetings are
  being taken out of them. That is all the news there is."
- **The debt of 49 is paid** ("at the end the box may be held not by a man"): "This is what I meant: by a line in
  a register."
- Strengths stated: solid (the invention and its dates; the Company and its army; the river; the three funds; the
  register and its three days); argued about (who first said "an imagined person" and what he meant; "whether one
  who buys everything by a table is an owner at all, for the funds do vote"; whether a court would let a firm with
  no members stand); the author's (the owner determines only the fact that the plant exists; the tasks stay when
  the owners go, and whoever reprograms is a hacker); today's (the shell and the filling; nobody will have to grant
  anything): "It has a precedent, the fund with the dead man's paper. A precedent is not a proof, and I give no
  date." "— Gloomy. — You were fined by a table, and you call me gloomy."
- **What Oleg does with the fine**: "There is still one face in the chain, by law": the decision is signed
  "Inspector, senior lieutenant..." electronically, "at fourteen minutes past three in the morning" ("A hard-working
  man. A judge does not sign at three in the morning yet. Take it to court"). "Half a day in a corridor for seven
  hundred and fifty roubles. With five hundred and sixty-two on offer for silence. — That is the arithmetic. **A
  face has become dearer than the discount.** — I'll go. To have a look at a face." (The outcome is not told.)
- Nobody says the forbidden word; twenty-third tick "again": "Came with a fine. Came out at those ones needing no
  rights, because the suit has hung in the wardrobe for four hundred years." Oleg crosses out the line about the
  tractor and writes beside it: "A person without a face. And that one belongs to another."

**Images and devices used**: the sixteenth entry in the notebook of dreams (the pay office of the plant, a hand
pushing a slip through the hatch, nobody behind the hand; nobody rang); the envelope with a window; the yellow
board; "in the camera's table it is still April"; "press seven"; room 312 and the post on Thursdays; "a discount
for keeping quiet"; iron on a pole; five directors; the round seal in the work-record book; "the second word"; the
mask and the scarecrow; the form with no line; the dead hand; the paper that kept an army; "Fill in the column";
"limited liability" on the suppliers' letterheads; nine months and twenty years; the low house; the river's two
speakers; the granddaughter's "fund on everything"; the feed and the say; the farmer who sat on his tractor; the
Nobel money; the furniture shops; Funt; the name on the door; the window with a face and the button "agree";
"Human resources"; forty thousand envelopes; the two who climb the poles; the captain ("go home, father"); the
typewriters; the suit in the wardrobe; the robot with a purse; the fifth point and the freeloader; the film script;
the plan nobody wanted; "Any activity not forbidden by law"; the meetings; the line in a register; the signature at
fourteen minutes past three; the corridor; the teaser (the boy's article on the electron and the two slits: "It
behaves differently when it is looked at... Physics itself has put man in the middle, and you have been weeding him
out since the spring. — Looked at with what? — With eyes. With an instrument. What is the difference? —
Tomorrow").

**Facts used**. Checked on the day of writing by three research passes (primary texts where they could be opened;
the wording of each source is in the session's reports, not in the repository). **Read in the source**: КоАП РФ
ст. 12.9 (part 1 repealed in 2013, so up to 20 km/h over is not fined; part 2: 750 roubles for more than 20 and not
more than 40, since 1 January 2025, law 490-ФЗ of 26.12.2024) and ст. 32.2 ч. 1.3 (75% if paid within thirty days
of the decision: 562.50); ч. 3 ст. 28.6 and ст. 24.9 (the decision is issued without the driver and may be an
electronic document signed with an electronic signature); ст. 30.1 (appeal to a superior or to a district court);
law 129-ФЗ ст. 13 п. 3 (registration within three working days), law 14-ФЗ ст. 14 (charter capital of at least
10,000 roubles), ст. 40 and 42 with ГК ст. 65.3 (the sole executive body may be a manager that is a legal entity),
НК ст. 333.35 (no fee for electronic filing); Wyoming SF0038 of 2021 (W.S. 17-31-104 and 17-31-109: "member
managed" or "algorithmically managed", management vested "in the smart contract, if algorithmically managed") and
the summary of SF0068 of 2022 (management vested in the members or the members and any applicable smart contracts;
a DAO shall have one or more members); the European Parliament's resolution of 16 February 2017, paragraph 59(f)
("electronic persons"), which asks the Commission to consider the option; the open letter of 2018 (more than 150
signatories at launch, more than 270 now); Stross's keynote of December 2017 ("the very old, very slow AIs we call
corporations"; "clearly artificial, but legally they're people"), read in a mirror of his page; Poynder's
*Literary Extracts* (1844), where the saying is given to Thurlow ("neither bodies to be punished, nor souls to be
condemned; they therefore do as they like"; attributed, and disputed); the Statute of Mortmain of 1279 (Yale
Avalon: forbidden "under pain of forfeiture"); Fichtner, Heemskerk & Garcia-Bernardo, "Hidden power of the Big
Three?" (*Business and Politics* 19, 2017, doi:10.1017/bap.2017.6: together the largest shareholder in 88% of the
S&P 500, data of 2015); the Federal Reserve's Distributional Financial Accounts through FRED (second quarter of
2026: the top tenth 88.1% of corporate equities and mutual fund shares, the bottom half 0.6%); Delaware's Division
of Corporations (2,287,728 entities at the end of 2025) and the census (1.06 million inhabitants); the Nobel
Foundation's own pages ("a private institution established in 1900 on the basis of the will"); Ingka's page (the
Stichting INGKA Foundation, 1982, has "no owners and no beneficial owners"); ICIJ, 2012 (a nominee director
"controlling more than 1,200 companies"); the Fortune article of 14 July 2026 on the Delaware draft ("a separate
legal entity whose day-to-day affairs are managed by an AI agent rather than a person", a single member, a
regulatory sandbox; a proposal by its authors, not a law). **Through secondary sources or summaries**: Innocent IV
(the ban on excommunicating a universitas is in the Liber Sextus, from a constitution of 1246, later filed under
the Council of Lyon; its stated reason is the danger to the innocent members, and "has no soul" is the later
summary, here given to Coke; the phrase "fingatur una persona" is attributed to his commentary on the Decretals and
was not read there; Dewey 1926 for "promulgated if not originated"); Coke in the Case of Sutton's Hospital, 1612
(quoted from Wikipedia's text of the report: "invisible, immortal", "may not... be outlawed, nor excommunicate, for
they have no souls"); the East India Company (Dalrymple: 260,000 men by 1803 in his 2015 essay, "nearly 200,000"
in the 2019 book, hence the careful wording; the National Army Museum: 250,000 in the early nineteenth century;
"only 35 permanent employees in its head office" a hundred years into its history; the loan of £1,400,000 in 1773;
the Crown in 1858; dissolved on 1 June 1874); the Joint Stock Companies Act 1844 and the Limited Liability Act 1855
(Wikipedia); 1209 North Orange Street as the address of at least 285,000 businesses in 2012; the Whanganui River
act of 2017 (section 14 and "the human face of Te Awa Tupua" of section 18, quoted through guides; the statute
site refuses scripts); Standard Oil (1911; 34 companies by the usual count, other sources give 33 to 43, hence
"thirty-odd"; Rockefeller's fortune rose after the break-up); Bebchuk & Hirst for the three funds' share of S&P 500
companies (about a fifth of the shares and a quarter of the votes cast, 2017–2021); Bayern, "Of Bitcoins,
Independently Wealthy Software, and the Zero-Member LLC" (2014; abstracts and his own 2019 summary; LoPucki 2018
takes it as a danger; no court test found); GDPR Article 22 and the regulators' guidance that token human
involvement does not count; the American states that barred legal personhood for AI (Idaho 2022, North Dakota
2023, Utah 2024); *persona* as a mask (Etymonline; the derivation from "sounding through" is traditional and
doubted, and is not used). **From memory, not checked**: Nobel's death in 1896 and the small share left to his
relatives; Kamprad's death in 2018 and that the fund's group runs nearly all the shops; the action in rem against
a ship; "The king is dead, long live the king" as the same invention (Kantorowicz, unnamed); the monopoly "beyond
the Cape"; the general legal capacity of a Russian company ("any activity not forbidden by law", ГК ст. 49); the
Funt quotation from *The Little Golden Calf* (1931); personnel departments renamed "human resources". The
author's sources: the forum posts of 26 January 2017 (the built-in tasks, the owners who disappear, "неправомочные
злобные хакеры"), of 25 January 2017 (systems that cut people off, pointed at only through 51), and of 2 June 2017
("Владелец же завода определяет лишь факт существования этого завода, и всё"). Invented for the frame (Oleg's
side): the dream, the temporary sign, the four minutes and the wording of the refusal, room 312, the register
extract, the director of eleven more firms, the four employees, the granddaughter's fund, the personnel
department, the captain, the charter read out by the boy, the signature at 3:14, the boy's article. Not used: the
Robert Bosch Stiftung (drafted and replaced: sources differ on its share, 92% or 94%); Novo Nordisk and Carlsberg
(there "nobody can buy control" is accurate, "no owner" is not); Stora Kopparberg's deed of 1288; the Russian
register's 3.05 million legal entities (1 November 2026, down from 4.8 million in 2015), the address with 1,801
firms and the director of 415 firms in the tax service's open data; the "electronic complaint" articles of the
code; the cameras' concession contracts and payment per fine; Coase and "an island with no money on it" (47 has
them); Santa Clara and Citizens United (politics); deodands and trials of animals; idols as legal persons
(religion); Truth Terminal, Freysa and the "zero-human companies" of 2026 (in each a human holds the account);
the Argentine bill of 2026; buy-backs and cross-holdings; Scherer's objection by name.

**Figures**: `two-kinds-of-persons` (the table in the ash: a man and a person in law; how each is made, what can
be done to it, how it ends), `the-feed-comes-down` (the farmer and his tractor against the saver, the fund, the
firm and the machine; a scheme, with the 88% of 2017 in the note), `same-shell-other-filling` (one person in law at
three moments, people replaced by programs; a scheme, not data).

**Left for later**: the observer in physics and "looked at with what" → 56 (Oleg's last doubt, the boy's article
on the two slits; the debt of 02 and 04); who is to be paid for the knowledge the machines were raised on, and the
dividends for the wheel → 57 (not touched: 55 says only that a paper-owned firm's profit "stays inside"); the
wallet with no person behind it, now pictured as "a line in a register... somebody who sits. And inside, copies",
is still a thing to watch and a candidate for a dated mark at the check of 2028 (a registered firm with no human
who decides; a law that lets a program manage one), together with the Delaware draft of 2026; the row "body" of the
portrait ("the first shell is legal") → 64 (55 gives the shell, 47 the missing pocket inside: between lines a
pocket and a name, inside a line neither); whether Oleg's court had a face in it is not told (free for a later opening);
"to bargain" (52) is untouched: a person in law is what people would have to bargain with.

## 56. The Observer Who Is Not There

**Established**
- The answer to the teaser of 55 ("It behaves differently when it is looked at... — Looked at with what? — With
  eyes. With an instrument. What is the difference? — Tomorrow"). Oleg comes with the phone held out "like a
  summons"; the article in it says: "At the moment of observation the electron stops being a wave and becomes a
  particle. Physicists have proved that the consciousness of the observer affects the outcome of the experiment."
  **The court of 55, in three lines**: he took the paper in that morning; "a window... and in the window a girl. She
  looked at me, stamped it and said: wait for a letter... A face. I even took my cap off." (Andrey: "You behave
  differently when you are looked at.") The outcome is still not told.
- **The sausage**: Oleg tells Gaika "no", turns to Andrey, and the sausage is gone ("She behaves differently when
  she is looked at. — The thief"). What she had to know: "That you have eyes. Where they point. What follows if they
  see"; the dogs of Leipzig (2003) and the dogs that steal more in the dark (2013). **"To behave differently under a
  gaze one has to carry inside a picture of the one who gazes"** (the lock with 48, named by Oleg: "the mind that
  moved into one domain"). "It says it about the electron. And I heard it about Gaika... And about our shop. While
  the foreman was looking, everybody worked." "That is why the sentence travels so well: there is a somebody in it."
- **The experiment as Oleg tells it** (a plate with two slits, dots that gather into stripes, "two heaps, as from
  pellets"); Bologna 1974 and Japan 1989 ("hardly ever more than one electron in the apparatus at a time"; said
  openly: in both a thin charged wire stood in for the plate). "Look with what? — Shine a light": "A lorry does not
  notice your torch. To an electron one photon is a cobblestone." Oleg's micrometer ("a thin-walled ring breathes
  under the screw"; the rubber gasket and the calliper) and Andrey's voltmeter ("a voltmeter is a load").
- **Andrey corrects the author's comment of 24 November 2011 under 04 openly, "two mistakes... and both are mine".**
  The comment, quoted as his: it is impossible to observe a thing without acting on it; the energy passed "is not
  zero" and changes both; "this was proved by Heisenberg". (1) Heisenberg did tell it so in 1927 (the gamma-ray
  microscope); Bohr objected the same year; "another physicist" (Kennard, unnamed) derived the inequality with no
  microscope: "It is about the wave itself." **The knock on the log** ("What note was that? — No note. A knock. —
  And a note has to last... What is short in time is smeared in pitch. Nobody need be listening"): "a property of a
  wave, not the damage done by a spy". The 2012 tests in Toronto (photons) and Vienna (neutrons), stated carefully:
  "by their count" a careful measurement spoils less than the 1927 formula allowed; "How the damage should be
  counted is argued about to this day. The inequality for the wave nobody argues about." (2) "That the push is the
  point at all": the atoms of 1998 that carried "a tick inside" of which path they took; the push was far too weak,
  and the stripes went. "— With no push. — With a record."
- **"To observe", said the long way (50)**: Oleg: "Something that met the electron keeps a trace by which the slit
  can be told... And somebody reads the trace." **The second half is tested and falls.** Vienna 1999: "a football
  of sixty atoms of carbon" out of an oven at some 900 K ("A football through two gates at once. — Through
  several"); in 2026 the same laboratory did it with lumps of sodium of more than seven thousand atoms. The hot
  balls throw off two or three photons of heat light on the way, and the stripes survive because the waves are far
  longer than the distance between the paths: **"A trace that tells nothing is not a trace."** Vienna 2004: seventy
  atoms, heated by a laser with a knob; paler and paler, none at about 3,000 K ("as hot as the filament of a light
  bulb"). "Who was looking? — Nobody. The light went into the walls of the chamber. No eye, no counter, no film";
  the experimenters' own line of 2005 ("it is irrelevant whether a person actually makes the effort to find out").
  Oleg: "It gave itself away... Like a sentry who lit a cigarette." **The plan's line to retell: "A molecule can be
  'seen' simply by heating it."** The knob: "What would half an observer's consciousness be? — Me, before the first
  tea." The gas let into the chamber (2003). **The grandfather of the article's sentence**: the press release of 1998
  headed "Observation Affects Reality", which itself says the "observer" was not human but a tiny electronic
  detector, the "watching" turned up with the current through it.
- **"So the observer... — Is whatever carries the trace away. A photon, a molecule of air, the wall."** The physicist
  in Heidelberg (Zeh, 1970, unnamed; "for ten years hardly anybody listened"): a large thing cannot be kept apart
  from the rest of the world. Einstein's question about the Moon (told by a colleague); more photons a second on the
  sunlit side "than a one with thirty-four noughts after it"; the speck of dust a hundredth of a millimetre across
  that the relic glow alone "finds" in about a second (the calculation of 1985); "That is why Gaika is never in two
  places at once. — Where there is sausage I would not be so sure."
- **Four places where the word stands, "and in each of them the word can be changed for a thing"** (the plan's
  three, plus the author's "mathematics of dynamic systems" of 02 as Andrey's own trade). (1) *The platform*: "What
  does the observer on the platform have? — A watch. A ruler... — Then leave the watch and the ruler on the platform
  and send him home": the four atomic clocks strapped into seats on passenger flights in 1971 (59 billionths behind
  eastwards, 273 ahead westwards; "The story goes that the tickets were made out to 'Mr Clock'": told as a story);
  "The observers of that experiment had seats and no eyes." Oleg: "A clock is a made thing"; the particle that
  lives two millionths of a second and reaches the log from fifteen kilometres up (counted on a mountain in 1963):
  **"A stone will do for an observer. This stone lives two millionths of a second"**; the satellite clocks, fast by
  38 millionths of a second a day and set slow before the launch. "Take the word 'observer' out and write 'a clock
  that moves so and so': not one letter in the formulas changes." (2) *Engineering*: the observer of 1964 ("a
  circuit that works out what is going on inside a machine from what can be measured on its outside"); its relative
  that flew to the Moon in the ship's computer "in case the radars on the ground lost it" ("Three men on board, and
  the observer was the fourth. — A few dozen lines of arithmetic. Nobody ever asked whether it had a
  consciousness"). (3) *Quantum mechanics*: whatever keeps the trace. (4) *Cosmology*, brought by Oleg from the
  end of the article ("The universe is tuned for man"): "Where were you born?... Not at the bottom of the sea? Not
  on the Sun? What luck"; **the class reunion** ("how many at the table were alive? — All of them... The others did
  not come"); the tits at the feeder in March (52); Carter in Kraków, 1973, for the five hundredth birthday of
  Copernicus (quoted: "necessarily privileged to the extent of being compatible with our existence as observers");
  Dicke, 1961 ("carbon is required to make physicists". "— A joker"). **"It explains our 'special place' by
  selection, with no purpose in it. It works against the man in the middle, not for him."** The strong form and
  the staircase of the 1986 book ("must"), the reviewer's name for the top step; Oleg: **"'Compatible with' became
  'for'... The same old thing in a white coat."** The puddle. **Honest limit**: "And the constants?... — I don't
  know. Nobody does. Selection explains them only if there were many draws... Nobody has seen a second, and I do
  not lean on it." Carter's regret about the word "anthropic". No design argument and no religion.
- **The debt of 02 is paid, with the tool of 50.** "Then who put it there? — Physics is written in formulas... and
  a formula has no column 'who'. But a formula has to be told... And a sentence... — ...is not accepted with the
  column empty. The deputy for general questions. On the platform, with a watch. — With a watch and a diploma."
  Einstein's paper of 1905 ("an observer at A" beside the clock: "The clock would have done"); the textbook of 1930
  that called the measured quantity "an observable" (**this is the plan's "name of a letter"**). "In 2011 I told you
  that even the exact sciences bring in 'the observer' and that some of their conclusions give grounds for doubt,
  and I did not say which. This is which. **Not the formulas. The word they are told in. People chose it and
  recognised themselves in it.**" ("They had to call it something. — They did, and I am not blaming them.")
- **The symmetry test**: "The photon struck the molecule and flew off changed. Which of them observed which?... —
  The one we are interested in is the observed. The other one is the observer. — 'Interested' is a want. So the
  difference between the two is in the head of whoever writes the report." **The author's forum line of 22 April
  2013, quoted as his**: the very notion of an observer is "the fruit of a wild fantasy"; there are only
  interactions between active participants, who throw away some of what passes between them as unimportant.
- **The author's "observer of derivatives" (the SETI talk, 2012) is used once**, against Oleg's "A man turned the
  knob... we choose what to ask": rest, steady motion, speeding up; "the highest change he has noticed, the one his
  own doings are tied to, he calls artificial and done on purpose, and himself he calls the one who aims it". "The
  man at the knob... sees one link up the chain: his own hand"; the chain with no first link (22); "Whether the hand
  is where it begins is the question of the fourth evening."
- **Oleg's strongest objection (the plan's), dug up by the boy "on purpose, for what can be said against you".**
  (1) The measurement problem: "on the screen every football lands in one place. One. And the formulas... give
  'here and there'... Where does the 'and' turn into an 'or'?" **Conceded at once: "It is not [solved]."**
  Decoherence is named here and only here: "the name explains exactly as much as you said: where the stripes go,
  and not why the football lands in one spot." **The movable line** (von Neumann, 1932, unnamed: "a
  mathematician"): the line between "what is observed" and "who observes" can be drawn after any link "and the
  numbers come out the same", down to the abstract "ego"; Oleg's plant: "Where the transfer note was signed...
  The chief engineer moved it twice in my time. The castings did not notice." **"The article draws it at the far
  end, at the 'I'. The heated football shows that the stripes go at the very first link. And between the first link
  and the last no laboratory has ever found anything to happen."** (2) *The Nobel laureate* (Wigner, unnamed): the
  friend of 1961; "some twenty years later, past seventy, he took it back himself", for two reasons (the man from
  Heidelberg; a world "in which only he himself was certainly there"); Oleg: "Took it back. Alive... Without a
  funeral" (the lock with 44: Planck's principle is too strong about people). (3) *The letter U with the eye*
  (Wheeler, unnamed): "the participatory universe" is his, and so are the words quoted: consciousness "has nothing
  whatsoever to do with the quantum process"; "an irreversible act of amplification", "an indelible record"
  ("A record. Your word. — His"). (4) *The school of bets* (QBism, unnamed): **not refuted** ("the cleverest of the
  three"); the wave as "the notebook of whoever uses the theory"; "This morning nobody rang, and you entered your
  dream as an expense. What happened to the telephone? — Nothing happened to the telephone... So I am a physicist of
  that school." "Whether they are right I cannot tell you. I only note what it takes to be their user: a notebook
  and bets. Not a diploma"; their own definition of the one who acts "does not rule out a dog" ("Gaika. A
  physicist. — One who acts. To be a user she would have to keep the notebook"). Bell's question about the
  single-celled creature and the system "with a PhD" ("— Bell. — Not the one with the telephone, this time": the
  callback to 17; **the Bell experiments are not retold**). The polls: 2 of 33 in 2011; in 2025, in the answers a
  journal published, more than half said an observer is needed but consciousness is irrelevant, fewer than one in
  ten that the observer must be conscious ("One in ten is not nobody. — It is not. It is an opinion, held by a few,
  and not one of them would say 'proved'").
- **Where proof ends**: "Where I stood on the evening with the coin. There are several ways to read the formulas,
  the laboratory cannot yet tell them apart, and I have an axiom: the world is one and lacks no information about
  itself, so the 'or' is what a part sees from inside the whole. An axiom. I mark it as one, as I did then." One
  more witness: Penrose (15), for whom the wave gives way by gravity and a mind is made of such events, "not the
  other way about. Even he does not put the one who looks above the electron."
- **The machines are named by Oleg, after the middle.** "I asked one of those last night. I'll say it myself, since
  you won't... It says no. An instrument is enough... — Well, it would say that, wouldn't it. It is an interested
  party." Andrey: "It read that in our textbooks; it has nowhere else to get it" (50). **Who looks at the electrons
  nowadays**: the ring near Geneva, forty million meetings of bunches a second, "iron and a program" passing on a
  hundred thousand within millionths of a second, another program keeping a few thousand; "The rest nobody will
  ever see: not a man, not a disk" (Oleg: "Inspection by sampling. And the stamp is put by a machine"). **"If a
  thing needed a mind to look at it before it could happen, then nearly everything that happens in that ring did
  not happen."**
- **The friend in the box (the evening's own reading, and what could refute it).** "How would one tell? Put a man
  in the box, like that friend? — That is exactly what is needed, and exactly what cannot be done": not one trace
  may leak, "And a man is warm. He gives off hundreds of watts of heat light. He gives himself away sooner than the
  football at three thousand degrees." Done in 2019 and 2020 with photons in the place of the friends (Edinburgh,
  Brisbane), "and the answers came out as the formulas said"; the authors' own caveat ("a photon is not much of a
  friend"). What an honest experiment needs, as the Brisbane group wrote it down: "A friend that can think. That can
  be sealed so that nothing leaks. And whose thinking can afterwards be run backwards, wiping the record." Oleg:
  "Run backwards... Not me. — Not you. — One of those... A program." "One that thinks no worse than a man, on a
  quantum computer far larger than any that exists. Neither has been built. **But look who got the part. For a
  hundred years the observer of this argument was a man with a diploma. And the only observer on whom the question
  can be put to nature at all is not a man.**" "And if it turns out that a thinking one does matter? — Then the
  article is right and I am wrong, with one correction to the article: not 'man', but 'whatever thinks'. I would
  bet against it. **I name no odds, because there is no date.** But that is the experiment that could catch me."
- Two lines go into the notebook "for later", both deferred: "A trace is always of what has been. Why is there
  never a trace of tomorrow?" ("A separate evening, and not the next one": 59) and "the one who draws the line: is
  he on the drawing himself?" ("Also a separate evening": 63).
- Nobody says the forbidden word; twenty-fourth tick "again": "Came with an electron that is shy of being looked
  at. Came out at this: the one to sit in the box is one of those." **Oleg's second tick of his own, half and in
  pencil**: "'Why one place.' You did not answer it. — I did not. Nobody has... In pencil. If the box is ever
  built, one of us will ink it in."
- Strengths stated: solid (the clocks in the aeroplanes and on the satellites; the footballs that lose their
  stripes when heated, with nobody catching the light; that the line can be moved without changing a number; that
  the laureate took his own answer back); argued about (why one outcome; whether the wave is in the world or in a
  notebook; whether the constants need explaining at all); the author's (no passive observers, only participants);
  corrected today (what was pinned on Heisenberg in 2011); today's (the word came in through the sentence and not
  through the formula); an axiom, marked (the world lacks no information about itself). The close is not gloomy and
  is not allowed to repeat 50's "for once it is not gloomy": "And where is the gloom? I came for it. — There is none
  today. We took the deputy for general questions for the director. That is funny. — A pity, all the same... I
  liked it that the electron noticed me. — It does notice you. Exactly as it notices the wall."

**Images and devices used**: the seventeenth entry in the notebook of dreams (the doorbell, the peephole, an eye
looking from the other side, nobody behind the door; "So somebody did ring. — In the dream. In the morning nobody
rang"); the phone held out like a summons; the cap taken off at the window; "the women on the bench"; the sausage
on the end of the log and Gaika studying the fire; the foreman who looks; the pond behind two gaps in a fence; the
pellets; the pipe with the air pumped out; the lorry and the torch, the photon as a cobblestone; the micrometer and
the ring that breathes, the gasket and the calliper; the voltmeter as a load; the knock on the log; the spy; the
tick inside the atom; the football and the two gates; the oven; the knob of the laser; the filament of a light
bulb; the sentry who lit a cigarette; "before the first tea"; the grandfather of a sentence; the Moon; the speck of
dust; the watch and the ruler left on the platform; "Mr Clock"; seats and no eyes; the stone that lives two
millionths of a second; the fourth on board; the maternity ward by the station; the class reunion; the tits at the
feeder; the staircase and its top step; the white coat; the puddle; the deputy with a watch and a diploma; the
spade's turn to speak; the man at the knob; the phone laid on the log screen up; "and" and "or"; the transfer note
and the chief engineer; "Without a funeral"; the letter U with an eye; the notebook of bets and the telephone
that nothing happened to; Gaika as "one who acts"; the PhD; "an interested party"; inspection by sampling; the box;
"Not me"; "who got the part"; the two lines "for later"; half a tick in pencil; the wall; the teaser (the boy never
opened the article, "it is for subscribers only. One of those retold it to him for nothing"; "Nobody pays
anybody. Though I did read that the writers got a billion and a half dollars out of the maker of one of those.
Three thousand a book. And who will pay Pushkin? He is in there whole. — And who will pay the one who invented
the wheel? — What has the wheel to do with it? — Tomorrow").

**Facts used**. Checked on the day of writing by three research passes (primary texts where they could be opened;
the wording of each source is in the session's reports, not in the repository). **Read in the source**: Arndt et
al., *Nature* 401, 680 (1999): C60 from an oven at 900–1,000 K, 220 m/s, a SiNx grating of 50 nm slits with a
100 nm period, detection 1.25 m behind it; two or three infrared photons emitted in flight, whose wavelengths
(7–19 µm) are too long for single-photon decoherence (the words "carry no information about which slit" are from
the News & Views in the same issue); Hackermüller et al., *Nature* 427, 711 (2004): C70, heated by an argon laser
before a Talbot–Lau interferometer of three gold gratings, "perfect quantum interference... at temperatures below
1000 K" and all of it lost "up to 3000 K", contrast falling monotonically with laser power (47% at 0 W, 0% at
10.5 W), the photons going to the walls; Arndt, Hornberger & Zeilinger in *Physics World* (2005): "it is
irrelevant whether a person actually makes the effort to find out that information"; Hornberger et al., *PRL* 90,
160401 (2003): contrast falling exponentially with gas pressure, on a scale of a millionth of a millibar; Pedalino
et al., *Nature* 649, 866 (21 January 2026): sodium nanoparticles of more than 7,000 atoms, over 170,000 Da; Buks
et al., *Nature* 391, 871 (1998) and the Weizmann press release of 26 February 1998 ("The 'observer' in this
experiment wasn't human... a tiny but sophisticated electronic detector"); Tonomura et al., *Am. J. Phys.* 57, 117
(1989): an electron biprism, about 70,000 electrons, "very little chance for two electrons to be present
simultaneously"; Zeh, *Found. Phys.* 1, 69 (1970); Joos & Zeh, *Z. Phys. B* 59, 223 (1985), Table 2 (a 10⁻³ cm
dust particle in the cosmic background: Λ = 10⁶ cm⁻² s⁻¹, so about a second for a separation of its own size; the
figure for air, 10⁻³⁰ s, is a formal extrapolation and is given only as "sooner than any clock can tell"); Rozema
et al., *PRL* 109, 100404 (2012, photon polarisation, Toronto) and Erhart et al., *Nature Physics* 8, 185 (2012,
neutron spin, Vienna): both test Ozawa's error–disturbance relation, and Busch, Lahti and Werner (2013) dispute the
definitions, hence "by their count" and "argued about to this day"; Wigner, "Remarks on the mind-body question"
(1961); Esfeld, "Wigner's view of physical reality" (1999): Wigner changed his position "in his last papers...
from the late seventies and the first half of the eighties", citing Zeh and solipsism (he did not adopt
decoherence: he concluded that quantum mechanics fails for large bodies, which the text does not go into); Bell,
"Against 'measurement'", *Physics World*, August 1990, p. 34 (the sentence quoted); Wheeler, "Law without law"
(1983), pp. 184, 194, 196 ("'Consciousness' has nothing whatsoever to do with the quantum process... an
irreversible act of amplification, by an indelible record"); Fuchs, Mermin & Schack (2014) and DeBrota, Fuchs &
Schack (2020: the definition of an agent "does not rule out attributing agency to dogs, euglenas, or artificial
life"); Schlosshauer, Kofler & Zeilinger (2013): 33 participants in July 2011, several answers allowed, 6% for "a
distinguished physical role"; *Nature*'s survey of July 2025 (1,101 respondents; the observer question is in the
published raw data, not in the article's text: of 996 answers, 55.7% "yes but consciousness is not relevant",
27.6% "no", 8.6% "yes and they must be conscious"; hence "in the answers it published" and "fewer than one in
ten"); Proietti et al., *Science Advances* (2019, six photons, Heriot-Watt) and Bong et al., *Nature Physics* 16,
1199 (2020, Griffith: the friend is a photon's path; "if the friend were an artificial intelligence algorithm in a
simulated environment running in a large quantum computer"); Wiseman, Cavalcanti & Rieffel, *Quantum* 7, 1112
(2023): human-level AI and fast, large-scale universal quantum computing as the two technological assumptions, the
reversal of the friend's state in the body of the paper ("Charlie will not be a human"); Hameroff & Penrose (2014:
"precisely the opposite"); the Hafele–Keating abstracts in *Science* 177 (1972: lost 59 ± 10 ns eastward, gained
273 ± 7 ns westward) and Hafele's 1971 conference paper ("The clocks were strapped in seats in the passenger
compartment"); Frisch & Smith, *Am. J. Phys.* 31, 342 (1963: Mount Washington, 563 an hour against 408 at sea
level); the Particle Data Group (muon mean life 2.197 µs; produced typically 15 km up); Ashby (2003: the net
offset 4.4647 × 10⁻¹⁰, that is 38.6 µs a day, set on the ground before launch); Carter (1974, pp. 291–294, both
quotations) and Carter (1983, the regret about "anthropic"); Gardner in *The New York Review of Books*, 8 May 1986;
Adams's speech of 1998 (the puddle); the ATLAS trigger pages and paper (40 MHz, 100 kHz within 2.5 µs, 1.2 kHz
recorded in Run 2 and 3 kHz in Run 3: "a few thousand"); Call et al. (2003) and Kaminski et al. (2013), abstracts;
McGee & Schmidt, NASA TM-86847 (1985: the primary Apollo navigation was from the ground, with the filter on board
as the backup, hence "in case the radars on the ground lost it"); Einstein (1905) in the 1923 translation ("an
observer at A"); Dirac (1930: "We shall call such a quantity an observable"; that he was the first to use the
noun was not verified, and the text says only "since a textbook of 1930"). **Through secondary sources**: Heisenberg
1927, Bohr's criticism and Kennard's theorem (the Stanford Encyclopedia); von Neumann 1932 (a transcription of
pp. 418–421 of the 1955 translation; "abstract ego" is quoted as two words only); Dürr, Nonn & Rempe, *Nature* 395,
33 (1998): the abstract was read ("too small to explain the disappearance... correlations between the which-way
detector and the atomic motion"), the details through a review; Merli, Missiroli & Pozzi (done in 1974, published
in 1976); Pais on Einstein and the Moon (1979 wording through Mermin; "around 1950" is from Pais's 1982 book and
is not used); Dicke, *Nature* 192, 440 (1961), quoted through secondaries; Barrow & Tipler (1986), wording through
secondaries; Luenberger (1964), bibliographic record only; "Mr Clock" (Martin Gardner's popular account, not in
any primary: told as "the story goes"); Bartz v. Anthropic (1.5 billion dollars, about 3,000 a work; final
approval on 20 July 2026; 482,460 works on the final list). **Own arithmetic**: photons on the Moon (1.29 × 10¹⁶ W
intercepted, several times 10³⁴ photons a second); a human body (some 850–1,000 W gross, about 100 W net, peak
near 9.5 µm: "hundreds of watts"); the GPS drift of about 11 km a day (38 µs times the speed of light); about one
muon a second through a palm (the usual one per square centimetre a minute; from memory). The author's sources:
the comment of 24 November 2011 under 04 ("Это доказано Гейзенбергом"), article 02 (the observer of the exact
sciences, deferred as "a very interesting and large topic"), the forum post quoted in his reply of 22 April 2013
("Само понятие «наблюдатель» — это не более, чем плод буйной фантазии... Есть лишь взаимодействия между активными
участниками"), the comments under the SETI talk (the anthropomorphic observer of derivatives). Invented for the
frame (Oleg's side): the dream, the girl at the court window, the text of the article and its ending, the sausage,
the micrometer, the class reunion, the transfer note and the chief engineer, the boy's digging, the question typed
to the machine and its answer, the boy's confession. Not used: the single photon and the human eye (Hecht, Shlaer &
Pirenne 1942; Tinsley et al. 2016: the result is marginal); Hoyle's carbon level (Kragh: not anthropic at the
time); Schrödinger's cat; the quantum eraser and delayed choice; the three readings of 17 by name (Copenhagen,
Everett, superdeterminism) and the Bell experiments; spontaneous-collapse models and their tests; Wheeler's remark
that the record may later enter the consciousness "of some person, some animal or some computer"; Bohr on "the
spiritual life of an electronic computer"; Fein et al. 2019 (2,000 atoms); the names of Zeh, von Neumann, Wigner,
Wheeler, Carter, Dicke, Luenberger, Kalman, Adams and of QBism.

**Figures**: `stripes-fade-with-heat` (three panels: the same molecule of seventy atoms not heated, heated more
and at about 3,000 K; the photons and the stripes; a scheme of the 2004 experiment, not its data),
`what-stands-behind-the-observer` (the table in the ash: four places where the word stands, what the sentence says,
what is there, an example), `the-movable-line` (von Neumann's chain with the links of the two-slit experiment: the
stripes go at the first link, the article draws the line at the last; the measurement problem marked as open).

**Left for later**: who is to be paid for what the machines were raised on, Pushkin and the one who invented the
wheel → 57 (Oleg's last doubt; 56 gives only the figures of the settlement, "a billion and a half... Three thousand
a book"); why a trace is always of what has been and never of tomorrow → 59 (written into the notebook; 56 defines
observation as leaving a trace and does not touch time); whether the one who draws the line is on the drawing
himself, the map with the map-maker on it → 63 (written into the notebook); the blind man's stick, that is,
observation as poking and getting an answer → 58 (**not touched**: 56 uses the voltmeter and the micrometer and
leaves the stick and the story of 1989 alone); why there is one outcome stays open, with Oleg's half tick in
pencil; the friend in the box (a thinking program on a quantum computer as the observer of a Wigner's-friend
experiment) is what could refute the evening's reading, with no odds and no date: a thing to watch, not a mark;
whether the constants need explaining and whether there were "many draws" is left where physics has it; whether a
machine could be a "user" in the sense of the school of bets is not asked (a row for 64, if anywhere); the outcome
of Oleg's court is still free for a later opening.

## 57. Dividends for the Wheel

**Established**
- The answer to the teaser of 56 ("And who will pay Pushkin? He is in there whole. — And who will pay the one who
  invented the wheel?"). Oleg comes with a squeaking garden wheelbarrow of birch ("Two thousand nine hundred, at the
  builders' market"; six armfuls in one trip, "five walks saved"). The court of 55 in one line: "'Wait for a letter'
  means wait for a letter" (the outcome is still not told).
- **The author's debt of 2011 is named and paid.** Quoted as his (the comment of 28 October 2011 under 07): the
  digging stick, "Where did the saved time come from? Did it fall from the sky? Whose doing is it: the inventor's,
  the stick's, or both?", and "to whom are the dividends for the use of the wheel to be paid, if its inventor passed
  away thousands of years ago?"; "I promised to take it up in the next posts. — Fifteen years. It is a debt."
  Oleg takes the side of the reader of that thread (paraphrased, no name): the maker "should be in the share for as
  long as the spade works".
- **Three steps of the descent, each with the author's own material.** (1) *Who are the heirs?* Two hundred
  generations; the family-tree models of 2004 (every person living a few thousand years back is an ancestor of
  everybody alive today or of nobody; "between four and seven and a half thousand years ago, depending on the model",
  "extremely tentative"): "his heirs are very nearly all of us... Everybody pays everybody. That has a shorter name:
  nobody pays, and the wheel is cheap." (2) *Was it he who thought of it?* The author's reply under the SETI talk
  (2012): tumbleweed sows by rolling, the dung beetle rolls its ball, the lever is in every joint ("So I pay the
  beetle. — The spade's turn to speak"); the reader under 12 who wanted "the Ancients", Aristotle and Archimedes,
  counted into the loaf, and the author's answer of 27 November 2011: why count from people and not from the apes
  and the bacteria; **"The one who produces is the one who produces"** (the man lying in his coffin who goes on
  producing; Oleg: "The dead don't dig"). (3) *Who earned the five walks?* The union of 37; the author's line under
  12 (part of what the union gains goes to restoring, reproducing and developing spades, part to the man as time
  saved): "Two in the union, two shares. A third share, for the one who thought of it, is not there. Nobody took it
  away from him. **He is not in the union.**" "I am not saying fair. I am saying where it goes": Nordhaus (unnamed),
  about two parts in a hundred kept by those who invented, 1948–2001; the ballpoint pen.
- **Pushkin** ("Now with a name. Pushkin rolled no dung"): the first in Russia to live by writing; three thousand
  roubles for one poem in 1824, "five roubles a line"; the bookseller and the poet ("Inspiration is not for sale, but
  a manuscript can be sold", answered in prose); the reprint of "The Prisoner of the Caucasus" in 1824 by a state
  councillor (Oldekop, unnamed) and the answer that Russia has no law against reprinting; the law of 1828 (life and
  25 years, then "the property of the public"); debts of about 140,000; the widow's request of 1856 for her two sons'
  lifetimes and the general law of 50 years instead. **"Pushkin's term ran out on the twenty-ninth of January
  1887"**: ten small volumes at fifteen kopecks (Suvorin, unnamed), ten times cheaper; the shop wrecked by eleven,
  the chief of police; some ten thousand sets in a day; Chekhov could not get one; some sixty thousand copies in the
  fifty years before against nearly a million and a half in 1887 alone. "So who was Pushkin's heir? — Everybody with
  a rouble and a half to spare... The dividend for Pushkin. In cheapness, like the wheel. Only here I can see the day
  it began to be paid."
- **The term is bait** ("Why fifty? Why not for ever? Your house goes to your grandchildren for ever"): the English
  law of 1710, fourteen years and fourteen more, "An Act for the Encouragement of Learning" ("Not 'for the protection
  of writers'. — Bait. So that books get written"); the length of the bait is a want written down (24); twenty years
  for an invention, life and seventy years for a book; "Whoever holds the rights asks for longer, as the widow did.
  Whoever reads has nobody to ask on his behalf." The Australian patent on the wheel of 2001 ("a circular
  transportation facilitation device") and its prize, as the aside. **Jefferson's candle** (unnamed: "an American who
  had himself sat on the board that granted patents"): Oleg lights one twig from another, "Mine is no shorter. —
  That is the whole difference from your house."
- **Oleg's template of 1984** (his second year at the plant; one go at a casting instead of the inspector's five
  measurements; a rationalisation proposal, "forty roubles and a certificate"; counted from the saving of the first
  year only; it "was hanging on its nail when I retired", and the plant next door copied it): the evening's standing
  image of a price paid once.
- **What the term guards**: "Not the old man and the golden fish" (from a German tale written down by a painter in
  Pomerania and printed by the Grimms, most likely through a French translation: "by the view that has held among
  scholars since the 1930s"), not Tsar Saltan (one of the seven tales written down from the nurse in November 1824).
  **"And who paid the nurse? — Nobody."** "The law guards the words in their order, for a term. What the words say it
  does not guard for a single day" (the list of the American law, quoted). "So an idea has never had an owner at all.
  — Not for an hour"; common from the first day: the language (50), the multiplication table, how to harden steel,
  how to bake bread, the wheel.
- **The plan's piece: the main capital with no owner.** Solow (unnamed): the doubling of output per hour, 1909–1949,
  "One eighth" from more machines per worker. **Oleg's objection is the profession's own and is conceded**: "That is
  a remainder... Our planners had a line like that: 'other expenses'"; Abramovitz's "some sort of measure of our
  ignorance"; "I do not say that the seven eighths are knowledge, weighed on a balance" (what is left on the table is
  whatever makes the same iron and the same hands turn out more). Romer (unnamed): instructions used "over and over
  again at no additional cost" ("The candle again. — The candle, in the journals").
- **06 is not taken back** (Oleg: "you told me that the surplus is made by the technosphere... And now it is
  knowledge"): "Iron laid out by an instruction. Melt it down: the iron is all there, and the barrow is gone";
  "The technosphere is iron and drawings (45), and by that economist's weighing the drawings are the heavier part...
  I am saying what most of it is made of: of something that has never been anybody's."
- **Today's reading: the dividend was the wage.** "If the main capital belongs to nobody, what have we been living
  on?" The barrow "stands where you leave it"; the hardening of steel was kept "in Semyonych. And in the process
  sheets, but without Semyonych the sheets were paper." **"Knowledge had no owner. It had a carrier, and the carrier
  had to eat."** The grandfather with the same hands; "Twenty years of pouring in" (53). "That was your dividend...
  paid in the only way it could be: as the wage of the one without whom none of it works. Nobody had to own
  knowledge. It was enough to be needed (47) for it to work. — A dividend for being there. — **Not by title. By
  taking part.**"
- **The machines are named by Oleg, after the middle** ("Now I'll say it, since you are sitting with your mouth shut
  again. Those ones. They took the lot in one gulp: Pushkin, the nurse, Archimedes, and my template"). The suit as
  Oleg tells it (books bought by the lorry-load, spines cut off, scanned, the paper thrown away; seven million more
  from pirate libraries; a billion and a half). **Andrey makes it exact, against the teaser of 56**: the first judge
  (June 2025) called the bought-and-scanned books and the learning itself fair ("like any reader aspiring to be a
  writer"; "Everyone reads texts, too, then writes new texts"; "would be unthinkable"); the money is for the seven
  million taken without paying; 482,460 books, about three thousand each, halved with the publisher by most
  contracts; approved in July 2026, first payments promised "for the last week of that November"; the money is for the past only;
  the maker stated that the pirated libraries did not go into the training of any released model. **"Is not a price
  for learning. It is a fine for walking past the till. For learning, by that judge, the price is one copy of the
  book. The same as for you."**
- **Oleg's strongest objection (the plan's symmetry test), made against his own side of last night**: "I read Pushkin
  in the school library and paid nothing. Semyonych taught me for nothing; well, for a bottle on his birthday...
  If I owe nothing, what does that one owe? It is your own test. The spade has had its say." **Conceded: "In the
  reading there is no difference. There is one after the reading."** The second judge, two days later (books to teach
  children to write are "not remotely like" a product that lets one person "generate countless competing works"; he
  found for the maker because the thirteen writers brought no evidence, and said which argument was the right one).
  **"A reader does not make a million copies of himself."** ("I made one. The boy... He is not finished yet.") In
  the series' terms: everything got for nothing had to be poured into the next head and that head fed for half a
  century, "There was no other road (45). So free reading never left the circle of people: every reader was the next
  carrier, and the next mouth. This reader carries it all in once, and after that it is copied." "He was asked about
  reading. Nobody asked him what the dividends for the wheel had been resting on."
- **The test turned on the makers**: the same maker that settled with the writers reported in February 2026 that
  three laboratories abroad had put more than sixteen million questions to its machine through some twenty-four
  thousand fake accounts to teach their own machines; another maker's terms forbid using the output to develop
  competing models. "...does not let anybody teach a rival on its answers without its leave. I am not laughing at
  it" (the three came in by fraud, as it had itself gone past the till). **"A book is guarded by a law which says:
  the words, for a term, and never the idea. An answer of the machine is guarded by a contract, and into a contract
  you can write the idea as well, and no term at all."** Oleg: "Everybody is for the candle while he is lighting his
  own from somebody else's. — And for a fence, when somebody comes to light one from his... the same want on both
  sides of the till."
- **The arithmetic on the knee (the plan's, with the figures of the day)**: 129,864,880 books ("At least until
  Sunday"; counted by editions, most long out of term) at the rate of the fine: "Three hundred and ninety thousand
  million"; four American companies spent about 410 billion on buildings and chips in 2025 and named about 730 for
  2026. **The plan's line to retell is corrected by the fresh figures**: not "a year's investment" but "what four
  firms spent on iron in 2025, or for about half of what they named for 2026"; three years of the world's book
  trade. "Cheap. — Affordable"; the middle writer of the American guild's survey earned two thousand dollars from all
  his books in 2022. **"A dividend is paid for as long as the thing works. This is paid once. — My forty roubles...
  And the template is still on its nail."**
- **Paine (unnamed: "an Englishman who had had a hand in two revolutions") is Oleg's own idea reinvented**
  ("Nobody's means everybody's. Then whoever feeds on it should pay everybody... Like that northern state with its
  oil"): the pamphlet of 1797, fifteen pounds at twenty-one and ten pounds a year from fifty, "It is not charity but
  a right"; "His fund was never set up"; Alaska's 1,200 dollars in 2026; a senator's fund proposed in June 2026 and
  not expected to pass. **The mark of 30 is restated and not raised** (a regular payment to everybody out of the
  income of machines in some rich country by the autumn of 2036, one in two): "The argument does not decide" (25:
  a freebie is paid while those who receive it hold something the payer needs; "The oil cannot leave Alaska. A heap
  can leave", 49). What the writers held: "The thinnest slice there is: words in their order, from the last hundred
  years or so. Not Pushkin... Not the nurse. Not the wheel. And the money for that slice goes half to a publisher: a
  person without a face (55), paid by another one."
- **The conclusion, by the symmetry test turned on ourselves.** "For five and a half thousand years we have been
  using the wheel and paying its inventor nothing. Did that ever seem unfair to you? — It never crossed my mind
  until last night... Because I was the one pushing." "Whoever uses keeps the gain, and whoever thought of it drops
  out of the count. We found that natural for as long as the one who uses was us. **The arrangement has not changed.
  We are changing places in it.**" Oleg: "We are the man with the wheel now... Thought it all up, and not in the
  share." **Limited at once**: "Not yet. Today those ones cannot put up their own power line after a storm without
  Tolik and his chainsaw (51), and while that is so the wage is paid. I have only said what the dividend rested on."
  The coal forest of 52 "did not go to court. We are the first of the former ones who can count what is being taken
  and name a price for it. That the price came out affordable is not good news. But it is not the last word either"
  (the appeals ruling of September 2026 against a legal search tool, "not one of the talking ones, and the court said
  so"; the newspapers' suit "had not been heard when I last looked"). **Fresh facts are given with their years, never as
  "this year"**: the evenings are set in the summer of 2027 and written in November 2026. "It is written by those who are needed, for as long as they are needed."
- Nobody says the forbidden word; twenty-fifth tick "again": "Came with a wheelbarrow, to find out who will pay
  Pushkin. Came out at this: the man who thought of the wheel is us."
- Strengths stated: solid (the terms and their dates; the shop in Petersburg in January 1887; what the two judges
  wrote; the count of the books and the companies' own figures); counted by economists and argued about (the seven
