  eighths, a remainder; the two parts in a hundred, one man's estimate; the family trees, tentative); the author's
  (the one who produces is the one who produces; the union's gain is divided between those in it); today's (the
  dividend for knowledge was the wage; the suit is a bill presented by title); a forecast with odds (the payment to
  everybody, one in two by 2036, not raised). The close: "Gloomy. — But objective. And your own dividend is still
  being paid: five walks a load, for as long as there is birch."
- **A declared interest is written into the footer** (both languages): the settlement discussed was paid by
  Anthropic, the maker of the model that wrote the article, and the report on learning from a machine's answers is
  Anthropic's too. In the dialogue no company is named ("the maker of one of those", "another maker").

**Images and devices used**: the eighteenth entry in the notebook of dreams (the school library, the pocket at the
back of the book, "due back in 1978. There is a fine", the book is his own notebook); the wheelbarrow that squeaks
once a turn, turned wheel up; the till, and who was not at it; six armfuls and five walks; the digging stick; "should
be in the share"; two hundred generations; "the girl at the till"; "Everybody pays everybody"; tumbleweed, the dung
beetle, the lever in the joint; "So I pay the beetle"; the step on which a man appears; the coffin; "The dead don't
dig"; the barrow that "got bought"; the ballpoint pen; "Pushkin rolled no dung"; five roubles a line; the bookseller
and the poet; ten small volumes at fifteen kopecks; the desk with the till on its side; "Everybody with a rouble and
a half to spare"; the house that goes to the grandchildren for ever; bait and its length; the widow; the patent on
the wheel; the two twigs ("Mine is no shorter"); the template on its nail and the forty roubles; the old man and the
golden fish; the nurse; words in their order; "other expenses"; the growth still lying on the table; "the candle, in
the journals"; iron laid out by an instruction; the barrow that stands where you leave it; Semyonych and the process
sheets; the grandfather's hands; "A dividend for being there"; the lorry-loads of books with the spines cut off; "a
fine for walking past the till"; the bottle on Semyonych's birthday; the boy as a copy that is not finished; the
next carrier and the next mouth; the candle and the fence; "Three hundred and ninety thousand million"; the word
under the first column, "Once"; the northern state with its oil; the thinnest slice; "By pushing"; changing places;
"the man with the wheel"; Tolik's chainsaw; the forest that did not go to court; the teaser (it has read every book
about barrows and never pushed one, "Loaded, downhill, in the wet"; what Oleg knew about casting after the
trade-school textbook; "It is dark already. How will you find the path with that thing? — By feel. The wheel tells me
where the roots are. — Tomorrow we shall start from that").

**Facts used**. Checked on the day of writing by three research passes (primary texts where they could be opened;
the wording of each source is in the session's reports, not in the repository). **Read in the source**: Bartz v.
Anthropic, N.D. Cal., order on fair use of 23 June 2025 (Alsup; Dkt. 231: training and the print-to-digital
conversion of bought books fair use, the pirated central library not; Books3 196,640, LibGen "at least five
million", Pirate Library Mirror "at least two million", "over seven million" in all; the bought books are "millions",
no exact count; all the sentences quoted), the preliminary approval memorandum (Dkt. 437: "no split is mandated",
"existing publishing agreements will likely dictate an even split"; claims about conduct on or after 25 August 2025
are not released), the final approval order of 20 July 2026 (Martínez-Olguín; 1.5 billion dollars, 482,460 works,
"about $3,000" a work, by Alsup's footnote about 3,100; Anthropic's representation that neither pirated dataset was
in the training corpus of any commercially released model; the files to be destroyed), the status report of 2
September 2026 and the settlement website (first payments expected the week of 23 November 2026, about 70% of each
award; whether they arrived is not confirmed, hence "are promised"); Kadrey v. Meta, order of 25 June 2025 (Chhabria;
the sentence quoted; summary judgment for Meta because the thirteen plaintiffs "presented no meaningful evidence on
market dilution"); 17 U.S.C. § 102(b); the Statute of Anne (8 Anne c. 19, the title and the terms; in force 10 April
1710); TRIPS art. 33; Solow, *Rev. Econ. Stat.* 39, 312 (1957: "87½ per cent... to technical change and the
remaining 12½ per cent to increased use of capital", "about one-eighth... seven-eighths", and his definition of
"technical change" as shorthand for any kind of shift; Hogan's 1958 correction to about 90% is not used); Abramovitz
(1956: "some sort of measure of our ignorance"); Romer, *J. Polit. Econ.* 98, S71 (1990: "used over and over again
at no additional cost"); Nordhaus, NBER WP 10433 (2004: about 2.2% of the social surplus, range 1.3–3.3%; US non-farm
business, 1948–2001); Jefferson to Isaac McPherson, 13 August 1813 (Founders Online, read through an archived copy;
quoted in modernised spelling; he writes "inventions", not "ideas", cannot be a subject of property; the letter
concerns Oliver Evans's patent on mill machinery); Paine, *Agrarian Justice* (the SSA transcription: the sums,
"ground-rent", "the common property of the human race", "It is not charity but a right"); Taycher, Google Books
blog, 5 August 2010 (129,864,880, counted by "tomes", that is editions; "At least until Sunday"); the Authors Guild
income survey of 2023 (median book income of all surveyed authors 2,000 dollars for 2022: per author and year, not
per book); the capital spending of Amazon (131.8 billion dollars in 2025, about 220 guided for 2026), Alphabet
(91.4; 195–205), Microsoft (118.0 by calendar quarters; about 170) and Meta (72.2; 135–145), from their releases and
calls of October 2026: about 413 and 720–740, own sums, the bases differ; Rohde, Olson & Chang, *Nature* 431, 562
(2004: the identical-ancestors point at about 3000 BC, 5353 BC or 2158 BC by model, "extremely tentative");
Pushkin's letter to Benckendorff of 20 July 1827 (the reprint of 1824, the three thousand offered for a second
edition, "не существует в России закона противу перепечатывания книг"); "Разговор книгопродавца с поэтом" (1824;
the line is the bookseller's); Azadovsky (1936) on the fisherman tale; Chekhov's letter of 23 March 1887;
Anthropic's report of 23 February 2026 (three laboratories, over 16 million exchanges, about 24,000 fraudulent
accounts; it also says distillation is a legitimate method when a maker distils its own models, which the text does
not go into); the Australian innovation patent AU 2001100012 (filed 24 May 2001, granted 2 August 2001, later
revoked); the Alaska dividend of 2026 (1,200 dollars, including a 200-dollar energy payment); the Soviet statute on
inventions and rationalisation proposals of 1973 in its 1978 edition (a rationalisation proposal is paid on the
first year's savings, not less than 10 roubles). **Through secondary sources**: the Russian copyright provisions of
1828 and the extension of 1857 (Pereselenkov 1909, from the ministry archive; Kommersant, 5 September 2020; Levitt);
January 1887 (*Novoe Vremya* as quoted by Kommersant and by the commentary to Chekhov's letters: fifteen kopecks a
volume, "ten times" cheaper, the wrecked shop, up to 10,000 sets sold in a day; the sources differ on whether the
crush was on 29 or 30 January, so the text says only "for that day"; the first print run is not given); no more than
about 60,000 copies of Pushkin in fifty years and 1,481,275 copies in 1887 (Aizenshtok and Pavlenkov, through
Levitt); the three thousand roubles for "The Fountain of Bakhchisarai" ("Russky Invalid", 8 March 1824, through the
chronicle); Pushkin's debts of 138,988 roubles (Belykh 2019; the mortgage not counted; who paid them is not said in
the text); the seven tales of November 1824 (the academic chronicle; that the first of them is the Saltan plot is
the usual matching); Runge and the Grimm tale; the Stationers' perpetual right before 1710; the Ljubljana Marshes
wheel (5,100–5,350 years) and the Bronocice pot; the Ig Nobel prize of 2001 (that Keogh was a patent lawyer is from
the writer's memory of the press reports); Thomson Reuters v. Ross, 3d Cir., 29 September 2026 (through a law blog);
the senator's fund of June 2026 (Roll Call); the clause of OpenAI's terms; the world book trade ("something like a
hundred to a hundred and fifty billion a year": commercial estimates, methods not public; WIPO gives 76.3 billion
for 28 countries in 2022). The author's sources: the comments of 28 October 2011 under 07 (the reader's "он должен
быть в доле, пока лопата работает"; the digging stick and "Кому платить дивиденды за использование колеса, если
изобретатель колеса тысячелетия назад почил в бозе?"), of 27 November 2011 under 12 ("Почему не с приматов, не с
позвоночных, не с прокариот"; "Производит тот, кто именно производит"; the macrosystem "человек с лопатой" and the
split of its super-profit), and under the SETI talk (the tumbleweed, the dung beetle, the lever in the muscles).
Invented for the frame (Oleg's side): the dream, the wheelbarrow and its price, the template of 1984 and the forty
roubles, the bottle on Semyonych's birthday. Not used: Newton's "shoulders of giants"; Salk and "Could you patent
the sun?"; the Web put into the public domain in 1993; Volvo's seat belt; Donaldson v. Becket; the Venetian statute
of 1474; the HarperCollins licence of 2024 (5,000 dollars a title, halved with the author) and the other licensing
deals; Getty v. Stability; the music labels; the Disney deal; Gartner's and the AI Index's totals; the names of
Solow, Romer, Nordhaus, Abramovitz, Jefferson, Paine, Alsup, Chhabria, Suvorin and of all companies.

**Figures**: `dividends-have-a-term` (bars on a scale of years: the wheel, a book of 1710, a patent, Pushkin, a book
today), `where-the-gain-came-from` (two bars of 100%: Solow's one eighth and seven eighths, Nordhaus's 2.2% and about
98%; the remainder is called a remainder in the note), `price-of-everything-written` (390 billion against about 413
in 2025 and about 730 planned for 2026; "paid once", and not a price for learning).

**Left for later**: a mind that has read everything and touched nothing, the stick and the story of 1989 → 58 (Oleg's
last doubt: it "has read... every book there is about barrows. Has it ever pushed one?"; the teaser ends on finding
the path by feel, which is where the plan's 58 opens; 57 does not touch the model of intelligence); the shares in a
discovery made by the pair of man and machine (left open by 53) → 60, not 57 (57 says only that the union's gain is
divided between those in it); the payment to everybody stays the mark of 30 for the autumn of 2036 at one in two,
to be checked with the list (67); things to watch, not marks: whether a court or a law makes learning itself (not
the taking of copies) a thing to be paid for, and whether it is paid once or for as long as the thing works; whether
the contracts that guard a machine's answers hold against other machines; "to bargain" (52) is touched from one
side only: the writers' suit is the first bargaining by title, and what else people hold is left for 65 and 66; the
row "knowledge" of the portrait (carried in once, copied, nobody's from the first day, guarded by contract and not
by a term) → 64; the outcome of Oleg's court is still free for a later opening.

## 58. The Blind Man's Stick

**Established**
- The answer to the teaser of 57 ("Has it ever pushed one?... How will you find the path with that thing? — By feel...
  — Tomorrow we shall start from that"). Oleg comes in the dark with a hazel stick and his eyes shut ("From the gate,
  blind. Two roots, your bucket, and a hole that was not there yesterday"); late because of a tick taken off Gaika
  "with a loop of thread". The court of 55 in one line: "I felt in the letter box as well. Empty."
- **The stick, by Socratic descent.** The root is felt "at the end of the stick", not in the hand (Descartes, 1637,
  unnamed: "a Frenchman"; the rod of 2018 in Lyon: struck where they could not see, people told where "just as is
  possible on the skin", and better when they did the striking themselves). The blind test with the mug: lying still
  the stick "said 'hard' and shut up"; **"A stick that lies still says almost nothing. It answers. Who asked?"**, and
  who chose the question.
- **Oleg's first objection: "I have eyes... It pours in by itself."** The eye jumps two or three times a second; the
  picture fixed to the eye with a suction cup (Yarbus, unnamed: "a physicist in Moscow", the 1950s) is gone in one
  to three seconds: "An eye reports changes." **"So my eye is a stick. — With a very quick hand."**
- **The plan's kittens, told with the correction** (Held and Hein, 1963, unnamed; Oleg: "The kitten again. The one
  whose eye was kept shut", 44; "Another one, and this one had both eyes open"): reared in the dark, three hours a
  day in a striped drum, one walks in harness, the other rides in a basket on the other arm and sees the same
  stripes; three tests (paws put out to meet a surface, the blink, the glass over a drop): the walkers pass, the
  carried do not. **"Blind. — No, and here the story is usually told too strongly"**: pupils and pursuit worked, and
  after two days of running free the carried ones passed (the plan's line to retell, "stayed blind with healthy
  eyes", is not used). **"It got the answers without the questions."** People "asked more gently": the blind in the
  dentist's chair with four hundred vibrators (1970): feelings on the back while the camera stood still, things in
  front of them once they turned it themselves ("The tickling moved out of the back and into the room").
- **The loop named, and the lock with 39 and 56**: "Poke, take what comes back, find what the one has to do with the
  other." The two cocks of 39: "Shut one in the cellar for the night... — That is a poke. And from the porch?";
  **"An omen is what one gets from the porch. — And a law is what one gets with a cock under one's arm."** 56 was
  half ("no observers, only participants"); "The other half: a mind is a participant that does not wait for the
  trace to be brought. It goes and knocks."
- **The story of 1989, told in full for the first time** (prompted by Oleg's wheel-tapper at the station, "A sound
  one sings", and Semyonych's "It sings wrong"): "In eighty-nine I made a man like that. Out of a DVK." A radio
  engineer at the computing centre of his institute and an evening postgraduate; an engineer's "two sticks", a
  generator and a probe; the dissertation: "to find a fault without knowing the device. No diagram, no mathematical
  model, not even what the board is for"; the program could feed a test signal into any point of a board and take
  the signals at all the others; it "kept count", built "images of faults", and "worked out for itself which pairs
  of points, 'poke here, listen there', told it the most" ("It chose its own questions. I had not told it which
  were the good ones. I did not know"); tried on a VEF radio ("At first I won. Then we were level. Then it had the
  answer before I had decided where to begin"). **The chill**: "Not because it was quick... Because I knew what was
  in it... there was nothing in there about radios." **The plan's epigraph**: "So what did you feel out there, with
  that probe of yours? — Myself." ("Give it as much, and it will manage. By itself.") The dissertation "was never
  written. That is another story, and not for this fire" (the clearance is not told).
- **"How to build a mind without understanding it"** (the open thread of the SETI note): "How it worked, yes; I
  wrote it. Why this pair of points gave away that capacitor, no... — I built conditions"; the author's 2015 answer
  to a physicist (Panov, unnamed): if the difficulty lay in understanding, the human mind would not have appeared
  either. "Nobody understood my head before making it. — Nobody. It was grown."
- **The model, in the author's words of 2015**, drawn in the ash: stir reality; take the response; recognise the
  images already in memory; find what goes with what (the correlator: new images); in the middle the memory of 19
  and the wants of 18. **"In 2015 I wrote it in capital letters: nothing more is needed. Today I would write it in
  small ones. But I would write it. The rest is how much."**
- **The tick** (Uexküll, 1934, unnamed), **with the modern correction said aloud**: his three signals and three
  movements (the smell of sweat: let go; hair: run about; warmth: bore in); "Today's biologists correct him: it
  does not jump from a branch, it waits in the grass and clings, and it goes by breath, shadow and trembling as
  well". "A poor stick. — A short one. But the loop is whole... **A world is as large as what the stick reaches and
  the sensors can tell apart.**"
- **The blind and deaf Einstein** (the author, 2015; with his "expensive car put away in a garage"). **Oleg's
  objection (the plan's): Helen Keller.** Her own words: before the pump, "simply making my fingers go in
  monkey-like imitation"; 5 April 1887, one hand under the spout and w-a-t-e-r in the other ("Two signals at once,
  on two palms. For a correlator that is a holiday"). The plan's answer ("A palm that somebody wrote on, and a hand
  under the tap. That is a stick") **with what is honest added**: "nobody has made my experiment with Einstein, and
  nobody should"; she had nineteen months of eyes and ears and kept one baby word, "wah-wah", for water; "She does
  not prove my sentence. She does not refute it either."
- **Oleg's second objection (the plan's): mathematics.** "With a pencil": a case tried, a guess dead; the lock with
  15 ("A computer in a vacuum. A mathematician in a vacuum could not do it either"). **The machines are named by
  Oleg, past the middle** ("Those ones solve olympiad problems, I have read. With what pencil?"). The continuation's
  half-rule for the teeth of 21's saw: the program that played itself at Go (2017), the machine raised on some
  eighty million problems answered by a proof checker, a program that can be run: **"Those are not the easy things.
  They are the things where the stick is free. — And a loaded barrow in the wet is dear."**
- **The talking machine is the kitten in the basket; Oleg's strongest objection is the continuation's and is
  answered slowly**: "it guesses the next word. Then it is shown the word and corrected... That is a poke and an
  answer. It is your loop." — "Does the next word in the book change because of what it guessed?... Then what did it
  stir? — Nothing... It was the one being asked." Sensors, a very good correlator, a memory, "and a movement that
  changes nothing in what comes next. That is the basket." **"It rode in the baskets of everybody who ever walked
  and wrote about it."** Arithmetic on the knee: more than fifteen trillion tokens (2024; another maker named more
  than twice that a year later), about eleven trillion words, forty-four million words a year for a reader:
  **"A quarter of a million years in the basket."**
- **21 is not taken back** (Oleg: "from the moves alone... it builds the board in its head"): "From a retelling one
  can restore whatever the tellers put into it, and that turned out to be far more than anybody expected." What is
  not in there: what the hands know and the mouth was never told (45; "Your barrow in the wet lives there"); what
  nobody has poked yet (53: "Beyond the edge there are no books"); and the dream of the opening ("It says a thing
  that sounds like floor... what answered was its own head and not the floor": 33, 50, and the degeneration of 19:
  "A retelling of a retelling"). Oleg's first casting: top marks for "metal shrinks as it cools", then "a cavity
  the size of a fist"; Semyonych: "Now you know." "And did you know a single word more than before? — Not one...
  I knew where the fist was."
- **Against himself**: the librarian of 1986 (Swanson, unnamed) who put two unread heaps of papers together (fish
  oil and the illness in which fingers go white in the cold) and "touched nothing"; the trial of 1989 on thirty-two
  patients helped those with the simple form. **"A place to poke can be found... Nobody knows how many such links
  are lying in the libraries. Perhaps a great many. But it became knowledge three years later, on thirty-two pairs
  of hands."**
- **"The stick and the lesson are in different rooms"**: in a conversation "it is the kitten that walks", and the
  head that answered is the same afterwards (33); "It can be given a notebook... But a notebook is not a head"
  (44). Mended from four sides, with facts dated by year: tools (a place to run its own programs, 2023; a screen, a
  pointer and keys, 2024; work of about a day carried through alone, doubling in under four months, "margins of
  error enormous", November 2026); how it walks when let out ("Like the kitten on its first day in the lit room":
  the shop of 2025 that sold cubes of metal at a loss, invented an account and promised to deliver "in a blue
  blazer and a red tie"; "we would not hire it"; by the end of the year the weeks at a loss mostly gone, and the
  onion contract; "My first casting... 'Now you know'"); laboratories (the robot Adam of 15, 2009; the talking
  machine joined to a robot laboratory in February 2026, six rounds, more than thirty-six thousand mixtures, a
  protein forty per cent cheaper; "People prepared the reagents, mended the procedure and kept watch. And it was
  one protein"); hands ("Slowly": five million factory robots at the end of 2025, "arms bolted to the floor"; some
  twenty-two thousand two-legged ones shipped in the first half of 2026, six in ten to shows, research and data:
  "They dance at exhibitions"); and "the one that closes the loop: what the stick finds goes back into the lesson"
  (the robot head that went on learning from its own attempts, 2025).
- **The heir's stick has many ends** (the third road of 45): a toddler takes about 2,400 steps an hour and falls
  seventeen times, "And every child falls for itself"; the 4,096 simulated robots of 2021 ("They fell for one
  another"); the driverless cars, 270 million miles by mid-2026. **Oleg's objection and its concession**: "In a
  drawn yard the mud is drawn as well. That is my dream again" — "A dream with rules. It is as good as its rules";
  "A casting cools for as long as it cools. And nine women in one month..." — **"The stick moves at the speed of the
  world and not of the chip. That is why of my three needles (27) the hands are the slowest, and why I count in
  decades. But a thousand castings poured at once cool no more slowly than one."**
- **The conclusion, and the lock with 42**: "Who tried the fish oil?... Who pushes the barrow, pours the casting,
  holds the dog while the tick is taken out? — We do... **We are the stick.**" "It says where; we poke and report
  what came back"; the lodger kept for one job: "Today that job is called 'to touch'." Limited at once: "Today the
  one who asks the question is still you" (53). **The thing to watch (not a mark)**: "'Cleverer', by examination,
  means the basket has grown. Watch in whose hand the stick is. Four things: who chose where to poke, whose hands
  did it, who read what came back, and whether it went into the head. Count how many of the four are a person."
- Nobody says the forbidden word; twenty-sixth tick "again": "Came with a stick, to find out what that one knows
  about a barrow. Came out at this: the stick is me."
- Strengths stated: solid (the rod; the fading picture; the kittens with two corrections, the second being the
  later experiment in which a carried kitten that steers its own cart with its head learns depth, "so what counts
  is who steers and not who has legs"; the girl at the pump; the games against itself); argued about (how much of
  the world can be got out of words alone: "a great deal, and not the barrow"); the author's (the four steps and
  the capital letters; the blind and deaf Einstein, "a thought and not an experiment"; the program of 1989, "which
  nobody but me ever saw working"); today's (the basket; the two rooms; "for now the stick is us"). **What would
  refute it**: "A machine that has only read, and that brings again and again things about the world which nobody
  knew and which hold when they are checked. Not a place to poke: the thing itself."
- **A declared interest is written into the footer** (both languages): the shop kept by a talking machine and the
  machine that works a screen, a pointer and keys are Anthropic's. No company is named in the dialogue.

**Images and devices used**: the nineteenth entry in the notebook of dreams (night shift, the light goes out, a rod
from the scrap bin, "wherever I tap, there is floor", straight across the pouring pit; nobody rang), which returns
three times ("floor wherever I put the stick"); the hazel stick and the mug found by tapping ("Tin. Hollow. And it
moved, so it is light"); the tick behind the hanging ear; the letter box felt by hand; the eye as "a stick with a
very quick hand"; the basket; "answers without the questions"; the tickling that moved into the room; the cock in
the cellar and the porch; the wheel-tapper ("Ding, ding, ding. Then: donk"); the generator and the probe as two
sticks; the VEF; the chill between the shoulder blades; "I built conditions"; four words in a ring in the ash;
capital letters and small ones; the magnifying glass; the car in the garage; one hand under the water and one under
the word; the pencil; "where the stick is free"; a quarter of a million years in the basket; the fist-sized cavity
and "Now you know"; the two heaps of papers; "I close the window"; the notebook that is not a head; two rooms; the
blue blazer and the red tie; the onions; "They dance at exhibitions"; falling for oneself and for one another; the
drawn yard with drawn mud, "a dream with rules"; nine women and a thousand castings; "We are the stick"; "to touch";
the teaser (the stick tells of a root "when I am already on it"; yesterday's supper, potatoes with dill, and
tomorrow's, "Whatever she makes"; "Which you do not remember either. — Not a thing").

**Facts used**. Checked on the day of writing by two research passes and own searches. **Read in the source**:
Held & Hein, *J. Comp. Physiol. Psychol.* 56, 872 (1963: ten pairs, eight in Group X reared in darkness to 8–12
weeks, then 3 h a day; 9 to 63 h to criterion, about 30 on average; the passive kitten in a gondola, head and limbs
free, no locomotion; paw placement, blink and visual cliff, the active ones 12 descents of 12 to the shallow side,
the passive from 4 to 8; pupillary reflex, tactual placing and visual pursuit present in all; the passive ones of
Group X normal after 48 h free in a lit room; the two pairs of Group Y are not used); Walk, Shepherd & Miller (1988:
passive kittens that steered a go-cart with the head, or watched moving toys, discriminated depth); White, Saunders,
Scadden, Bach-y-Rita & Collins, "Seeing with the skin" (1970: 400 vibrators in the back of a dental chair; "With
fixed camera, Ss report experiences in terms of feelings on their backs, but when they move the camera... externally
localized objects in front of them"); Descartes, *La Dioptrique* (1637), first discourse (the sixth-sense sentence
read in French; the walk at night without a torch is given from the writer's memory of the same passage); Keller,
*The Story of My Life* (1903), chapters I and IV, and Sullivan's letter of 5 April 1887 (all the words quoted; the
thirty words of that day and the mug–milk discrepancy are not used); Uexküll in Schiller's translation (the three
cues are butyric acid, the touch of hair and warmth; the eighteen years without food at Rostock are his unsourced
claim and are not used); Adolph et al., *Psychol. Sci.* 23, 1387 (2012: 2,367.6 steps and 17.4 falls an hour); the
abstract and supplement of Miller et al., *Nature* 561, 239 (2018: "just as is possible on the skin"; more accurate
in active sensing; the rod's length and the comparison with the forearm could not be read and were taken out of the
text); Meta's post of 18 April 2024 ("over 15T tokens") and the Qwen3 report of May 2025 (36 trillion); OpenAI's
post of 23 March 2023 (a sandboxed Python interpreter); Anthropic's of 22 October 2024 (computer use), of 27 June
2025 and 18 December 2025 (the shop: tungsten cubes sold below cost, the hallucinated account, the blazer and tie,
"we would not hire Claudius"; "weeks with negative profit margin were largely eliminated"; the onion futures
contract; no profit figure is given and none is quoted); METR's post of 20 November 2026 (a time horizon "up to 27
hours" on the verifiable subset, doubling about every 3.6 months, very wide intervals); Hubert et al., *Nature* 651,
607 (AlphaProof: about 80 million formal problems, the hardest problem of IMO 2024); OpenAI and Ginkgo, 5 February
2026 (six rounds, over 36,000 reaction compositions, 40% lower production cost of one protein, sfGFP; people
prepared reagents and supervised: a company post with its own preprint); the IFR release of 24 September 2026 (a
stock of 5 million industrial robots in 2025, over 600,000 installed); Counterpoint, 20 August 2026 (over 22,000
humanoids shipped in the first half of 2026, over 60% to entertainment, data collection and research, 13% to
manufacturing; other trackers differ by up to a factor of two, hence "by one count"); Physical Intelligence, arXiv
2511.14759 ("more than doubles task throughput and roughly halves the task failure rate" on the hardest tasks);
Rudin et al., CoRL 2021 (4,096 simulated robots, under four minutes on flat terrain, twenty on rough, one GPU);
Waymo's safety page (271.3 million rider-only miles through June 2026). **Through secondary sources**: Yarbus
(*Eye Movements and Vision*, 1965/1967, p. 59: "all visible differences disappear after 1–3 sec", quoted in Tatler
et al. 2010; fading is slower for faint central images and perfect stabilisation is never reached); two to three
saccades a second; AlphaGo Zero's 4.9 million games (the 100–0 and the three days are in the abstract and the
maker's post); King et al., *Science* 324, 85 (2009: twenty hypotheses about genes for thirteen orphan enzymes,
twelve supported; "confirmed... through manual experiments" is in the abstract); Swanson (1986) and DiGiacomo et al.
(1989: 32 patients, improvement in primary Raynaud's only); the modern account of how the tick finds a host (carbon
dioxide, vibration, shadow, warmth; it does not drop from branches); fleet learning at Waymo; wheel tapping as a
trade. The author's sources: the comments of 24–25 July 2015 under the SETI talk (the six components and the four
steps; "пощупать реальность палкой слепца"; "НИЧЕГО БОЛЬШЕ НЕ НУЖНО"; the DVK-4 system of 1989, "холодок между
лопаток", "мой собственный 'принцип действия' — точно такой же"; "Если бы в этом была проблема, то и ЧИ тоже бы не
появился"; "Дорогой автомобиль, поставленный в гараж на длительную стоянку"; "Никакой супер-Эйнштейн, будучи слепым,
глухим, ничего не чувствующим..."; the Turing machine "оторвана от реальности") and the chapter of his prose on the
dissertation (the VEF-202 test rig; there the computer is a DVK-2, in the comment a DVK-4: the text says only "a
DVK"). His 2015 remark that the starfish robot ran "on an IBM supercomputer" is wrong (the 2006 paper mentions
neither) and is not repeated. Invented for the frame (Oleg's side): the dream, the stick and the mug, the tick, the
wheel-tapper at the station, the first casting and "Now you know", the chief engineer's nine women; on Andrey's
side, the course of the contest with the program ("At first I won. Then we were level"), which the author gives
only as its outcome. Not used: the Zagorsk school and its four graduates (they lost sight and hearing late, and the
popular account was disputed in 1988), Skorokhodova, Rovee-Collier's mobile and Watson's smiling infants, Polanyi's
probe, the macaques with a rake, LeCun's arithmetic of the four-year-old (he has changed both sides of it), the
results on causal questions and physics problems put to language models, A-Lab (corrected in January 2026 from 41
of 58 "novel" compounds to 36 of 57, "new to the platform, not necessarily new to science"), Coscientist, Robin,
Lila, the co-scientist, the IMO results of 2025 and 2026, the Erdős problems, the names of all people and
companies.

**Figures**: `two-kittens-one-view` (the carousel from above and the three tests of Group X, with the note that the
carried kittens were not blind and caught up in two days), `loop-of-the-stick` (the author's four steps around the
memory, with the program of 1989 in blue and what is left when a link is taken out), `who-has-the-whole-loop` (the
table: the two kittens, the program of 1989, a talking machine while it learns, in a conversation, and with tools
whose experience goes back into learning).

**Left for later**: why a trace is always of yesterday and never of tomorrow → 59 (Oleg's last doubt: the stick
tells of a root "when I am already on it", and "tomorrow is already there" of 17; 58 does not touch time); whether
what such a machine finds with its own stick can be understood by people, and that our images come from the world
we felt out in childhood → 60 (58 says only that a world is as large as what the stick reaches); the stick that
reaches other planets → 61 (not touched); play as cheap poking (36) is not drawn in; a row "senses and hands" for
the portrait (the loop closed, many ends, experience copied, the speed of the world as the limit) → 64; things to
watch, not marks: in whose hand the stick is, by the four questions; whether a machine that has only read brings
new facts about the world that hold (the stated refutation); the hands stay the mark of 27 (more than 8 million
industrial robots by the autumn of 2031; 58 gives the count of 5 million for the end of 2025 and does not check
the mark); the outcome of Oleg's court is still free for a later opening.

## 59. A Memory of Tomorrow

**Established**
- The answer to the teaser of 58 ("Why do I remember yesterday's supper and not tomorrow's?... Whatever she
  makes"). Oleg comes with the supper known "down to the bay leaf" (mushroom soup with barley) and a parcel: the
  shards of his blue cup, dropped by his wife while drying up ("seven shards and a handle"). The question, from 17
  and "in the notebook since the electron" (56): "a book I can open at either end. Why do I remember in one
  direction only?"
- **A trace and a preparation.** The eaten soup is in five places (in Oleg, the pot, the plate, the smell, the
  head), "and each of them says the same, and exactly"; tomorrow's supper is in one, a chicken thawing in the
  sink, and it says "roughly". The plate is the trace of 56; the chicken Oleg names "a preparation".
- **The film, by Socratic descent.** One billiard stroke with the cue and the slowing cut off cannot be told
  forwards from backwards; the break of the pyramid can ("I would laugh... all together they would have had to
  agree beforehand"; he begins to laugh "somewhere around five" balls): **"So the difference is not in the law of
  the blow. — It is in the count."** The cup backwards: "Which of those things is forbidden? — Separately, I
  suppose, none"; "The equations of mechanics do not tell yesterday from tomorrow."
- **The count** (arithmetic at home, checked): a new deck of thirty-six comes from the factory by suits; 36 cards
  lie in a row in "a thirty-seven with forty noughts" ways; the world is "a four with seventeen noughts" seconds
  old; shuffling once a second from the beginning covers "one part in a million billion billion". **"You are not
  shuffling towards a mess. You are shuffling anywhere at all. There is simply almost nothing there except mess."**
  Boltzmann, 1877 (unnamed: "a physicist in Vienna"); entropy is introduced as the logarithm of the count, the
  second law as "your shuffling". **"So it is not a ban. It is bookkeeping"**: the bead of 2002 that ran the wrong
  way over short stretches; a deck of two cards; the cup and the floor hold more molecules than a 1 with 25 noughts.
- **The trace, said the long way (the test of 50).** Gaika's paw print in the ash: "How do you know that she walked
  there, and not that she will?" Oleg catches himself: "I am explaining time by the word 'before'", and restates it
  by pages of the printed book: the print is on every page on one side of the page with the paw, "the side where
  the ash is more trampled". A record needs a clean place (smooth ash; the notebook's "empty pages. About thirty"),
  and "a clean page is a deck by suits"; the push went into heat. **"A print is a small order, bought with a clean
  place and paid for with heat, and heat is shuffling."** Landauer, 1961 (unnamed; the lock with 29) and the
  measurement of 2012 "again on a bead in water". **"A bill can be paid only towards the end where the shuffling is
  greater... That side is the one people named the past." — "So it is not 'traces remain from the past'. It is
  'the past is the side the traces are from'. — The first is how it is said. The second is what it means."** The
  head is "a print like any other, and a dear one: twenty watts" (35).
- **Cause and effect** (the author, 24 April 2013: "causes determine effects no more than effects determine
  causes", and the second law as the factor that begets such links). One end fixes the other both ways: the demon
  of 17 from the fall, an inquiry from the shards (the ladle that came off the crane, "put together to the second").
  The difference: "One thing at one end, a crowd at the other, and the whole crowd tells the same story"; the
  stone in the pond and the shore that would have to push at once. **"What we call the cause is the end of the
  link where things are gathered: fewer of them, and in better order. The effect is the end where it has spread."**
  The lock with 39: no "common effect" makes two things agree ("Two streams run into one river, but they did not
  agree about it"); Reichenbach, unnamed ("In the 1950s a philosopher noticed that the fork always opens one
  way"). The vase of 17 stands; added: "on the vase one can tell which end is which. By where the prints lie."
- **The exposure.** Oleg: "Yesterday cannot be changed, and tomorrow can." The choice of 17 is not taken away
  (real, not free). "Open for whom? — For me. — About which of the two do you have a plate, and about which a
  chicken? — **'Open' means that I have no trace.**" It is the lack of information of 17 "under a prouder name";
  the author's 2013 margin notes on Deutsch (unnamed: "a physicist's book"): "anthropocentrism has crawled out
  again", then "hurrah, common sense has returned". "Time flows" — "At what speed? — A second per... Per second.
  That is not a speed. That is a one." The author's 2011 sentence (the comment of 21 December 2011 under 14): we
  are drawn in the frames of a script frozen in eternity as perceiving the earlier frames and not the later ones;
  said the long way: "as carrying prints of the frames on the side of order... A property of the one who keeps
  the records, ascribed to the world. — The weed. — An old acquaintance."
- **The definition the series did not have** (the plan's). The chicken tells: "Roast, with garlic. She boils on
  Sundays, and tomorrow is Thursday... Forty years of suppers": tomorrow's supper is made "out of traces. And a
  rule: what comes after what". The 2011 sentence goes on (able "to make certain suppositions about the frames of
  the future"). The sunrise: "Did you remember that, or foretell it? — I cannot find the difference" — **"When the
  rule is good, a forecast cannot be told from a recollection."** The flowers that close before the rain (03, the
  author's example): "A trace of what has been, and a rule written into it by all the flowers that did not close
  in time." **"A mind is a memory turned the other way. — A memory of tomorrow. — The only one that nature
  permits. It is made out of prints of yesterday, so it costs nothing extra. And it is made by the head and not by
  the event, so it can be wrong."**
- **What memory is for**: "What can you do with yesterday's soup? — Nothing... — Then why does the head pay twenty
  watts to keep it? — So as to know what the chicken will be"; "The wants do not pay for a museum" (18, 19). The
  man of 1981 (K.C., unnamed) who lost every recollection of his own life and could not say what he would do
  tomorrow, "the same emptiness"; the five in London (2007) asked to imagine a beach: scraps "and no place in
  which it all stands". "So yesterday and tomorrow are kept in one cupboard. — They are made on one machine tool"
  (and yesterday is put together afresh too: the report of 50).
- **The prophetic dream of 39, in one exchange** (the plan's): "A claim to more than a forecast. A prophetic dream
  says: I was not put together out of yesterday, tomorrow pressed me... the dream turned out to be a forecast that
  had forgotten what it was made of." The wager of 39 is kept and sharpened: ten calls, and "tomorrow can press,
  and I go for firewood and rewrite this evening. I do not say 'forbidden'. A cup is not forbidden to jump back
  either."
- **The machines are named by Oleg, past the middle** ("Tomorrow, rain from three... I read that the weather is
  now done by those ones"). The weather network of 2023 (GraphCast, unnamed): about forty years of records, "not
  one equation of the air", ten days in under a minute, more exact "on nine measures in ten" than the best
  physical program; in 2025 the European centre put its own network on duty beside it. **Said honestly**: the
  records it learned from and the first page of every forecast are prepared by the old physical program. "A
  memory turned the other way. Made of iron." The talking machine is the same (58: guess the next word): "All
  the traces people have left in writing, turned forwards"; asked what year it is with nothing to look at, it
  names the last year it read much about: **"a letter that does not know when it will be opened"** (Oleg: "My
  grandmother's clock. The weight hangs still, and the pendulum goes"); with a stick (58), a calendar, it finds
  out.
- **Whose memory of tomorrow reaches further**: it depends "on how many traces there are and how good the
  correlator is" (33). Oleg's knee that aches before rain: "a good instrument. It knows one yard." **The bank**:
  the loan for the greenhouse answered in a minute from "thirty years of pay, the bills, the fine for the fence"
 ; "Whose memory of your tomorrow was the better one? — Theirs, I suppose. They gave it... **So
  the traces are mine, and the memory of my tomorrow is theirs.**" 03 restated: a mind is measured "by how far its
  memory of tomorrow reaches, and how seldom it is wrong. Whoever remembers tomorrow further acts sooner."
- **The ceiling is not in the head**: the week or two of weather (17) "the network has not moved by much"; and
  the forecaster "is himself in the book. A forecast that has been heard becomes one of the causes" (17: the bank
  forecast that Oleg would pay, "and so there is a greenhouse, and so I pay").
- Nobody says the forbidden word; twenty-seventh tick "again": "Came with yesterday's soup. Came out at this: my
  tomorrow is remembered better by those ones."
- **Oleg's strongest objection is held back until after the tick, and it is Loschmidt's (1876, unnamed: "his own
  colleague")**: by the same count a half-shuffled deck was more shuffled a minute ago as well; "The count does
  not know which way I am asking." Conceded whole: "The count alone gives no direction." The direction comes
  "from one fact added to the count: at one end the deck lay by suits. — The factory": the past hypothesis (named
  in 2000); Penrose's one part in ten to the power of ten to the power of 123, "in the very book you once brought
  me" (15); "hot soup, the same everywhere" is a mess for a gas in a jar and an order for matter that pulls itself
  together; the earliest light even to a hundred-thousandth; **the debt of 35 is paid as far as it can be** ("the
  hot Sun in the cold sky, which I owed you from the evening of the pot. The whole of our flow is that deck being
  shuffled"). **The plan's objection and answer**: "So you have moved the riddle. From my kitchen to the beginning
  of the world... — I have moved it. And there it lies for everybody: nobody knows." Boltzmann's way out (an
  accidental piece of order) and the answer by his own count: one head "with a recollection of soup that never
  was"; Andrey does not believe it ("a count that makes a recollection of soup more likely than the soup saws off
  the branch it sits on") and takes the factory "as a fact with no explanation". His old answer of 2011 ("why"
  asks for a cause, causes live inside time, the page before the first) is marked: "I cannot prove that this takes
  the riddle away. It is an axiom, the same one as in the autumn."
- **Hegel (the open thread of 08)**: "One sentence, and I have never unfolded it. I cannot unfold it honestly
  today either, so it stays a sentence. I shall not put a filling into it after the fact."
- **Oleg's third tick of his own**, half and in pencil: "Who laid the deck by suits"; "If it is ever answered,
  one of us will ink it."
- Strengths stated: solid (the count of the cards; the second law as a count, with the bead; the price of a clean
  cell; K.C. and the five in London, "who were not all alike: one of them managed"; one correction to "mechanics
  does not tell": the lopsidedness found in 1964 and seen directly in 2012, which "lives in rare particles and
  breaks no cups"); accepted by most and argued in the details (that memory is tied to the side of order;
  Mlodinow and Brun, 2014, unnamed); open for everybody (why the deck lay by suits); the author's (the second law
  begets cause and effect, "said in one paragraph on a forum"; the frozen script; Hegel); today's ("a mind is a
  memory turned the other way... a way of saying, not a theorem"). **What would refute it**: "A record of tomorrow
  that could not have been put together out of yesterday. Your ten calls would do."
- **A declared interest is written into the footer** (both languages): the talking machine that does not know
  what year it is is a machine of the same kind as the model that wrote the evening.

**Images and devices used**: the twentieth entry in the notebook of dreams (the grandmother's wall clock with
weights: the weight pulled up does not come down, "and the pendulum goes all the same"; nobody rang), which returns
once (the letter that does not know when it is opened); the letter box looked into "with my eyes now"; the blue
cup, the seven shards and the handle, the parcel left on the log ("Bury it yourself"); the soup in five places and
the chicken in the sink; the trace and the preparation; the billiard stroke and the break of the pyramid; the tea
towel that flies back with the cup; the deck of thirty-six, "fool", the factory; the paw print; "before" caught;
the pages of the printed book; the empty pages of the notebook; a record as a paid bill; the ladle and the
commission; the stone in the pond; two streams and one river; the vase with prints on it; fishing; "a second per
second... a one"; the weed; roast on Thursday, boiled on Sundays; the sunrise a little after four; the flowers
before the rain; the museum; the white beach; one cupboard and one machine tool; a forecast that forgot what it was
made of; the telephone with rain from three; the knee that knows one yard; the telephone screen down; the
greenhouse loan; "the flower has closed, and you are still looking at the sky"; the half-shuffled deck a minute
ago; the number that cannot be written; hot soup the same everywhere; one head and no soup; the branch sawn off;
the page before the first; the teaser ("Which you remember? — Roughly. The chicken is in the sink").

**Facts used**. **Read in the source on the day of writing** (abstracts): Wang, Sevick, Mittag, Searles & Evans,
*Phys. Rev. Lett.* 89, 050601 (2002: a colloidal particle in an optical trap translated relative to the water;
"entropy consumption can occur over colloidal length and time scales"; the text gives no duration); Bérut et al.,
*Nature* 483, 187 (2012: a single colloidal particle in a double-well potential; the mean dissipated heat saturates
at the Landauer bound); Hassabis, Kumaran, Vann & Maguire, *PNAS* 104, 1726 (2007: patients with bilateral
hippocampal damage "markedly impaired" at imagining new experiences, which "lacked spatial coherence"); Lam et al.,
arXiv 2212.12794 (GraphCast: trained from reanalysis data, 10 days at 0.25° "in under one minute", better than the
most accurate operational deterministic system on 90% of 1,380 targets); Lees et al. (BaBar), arXiv 1207.5832 (the
first direct observation of T violation, 2012); Mlodinow & Brun, arXiv 1310.1095 (the psychological arrow aligns
with the thermodynamic one "even if the memory system itself is completely reversible and non-dissipative");
Wikipedia for Loschmidt (1876), the past hypothesis (the term coined by David Albert in 2000), Kent Cochrane (the
motorcycle accident of 1981; semantic memory intact, no episodic memory, unable to say what he would be doing
later), ECMWF (in 2025 the AIFS became its first purely data-driven operational model). **Computed**: 36! ≈
3.72·10⁴¹; 13.8 billion years ≈ 4.35·10¹⁷ s; their ratio ≈ 8.5·10²³. **From the writer's memory, not re-read**:
Boltzmann's counting of 1877; Landauer (1961, IBM); Penrose's 1 in 10^(10^123) (*The Emperor's New Mind*, 1989);
the smoothness of the microwave background to about 1 part in 100,000; the Boltzmann brain argument; Reichenbach's
fork (*The Direction of Time*, 1956); CP violation in neutral kaons (1964); that one of the five London patients
performed normally; K.C.'s chess and his "same kind of blankness" (Tulving, 1985); GraphCast's training on ERA5
for 1979–2017 at six-hour steps and its start from the ECMWF analysis; that the practical horizon of weather
forecasts stays near two weeks; that language models asked the date tend to name a year near the end of their
training data; a cup and a patch of floor hold more than 10²⁵ molecules (own estimate). The author's sources: the
comment of 21 December 2011 under 14 ("Мы, люди, являемся нарисованными в кадрах застывшего в вечности сценария.
И мы 'изображены' в этих кадрах воспринимающими предыдущие кадры... и не воспринимающими последующие, но по
отслеживанию математических зависимостей между содержимым кадров, могущими делать определенные предположения о
содержимом кадров из будущего"; "За пределами времени законы причинно-следственных отношений не работают"); the
forum, 24 April 2013 ("Причины определяют следствия не больше, чем следствия определяют причины"; "Такая
взаимосвязь проявляется в силу действия ВНТ... ВНТ становится тем самым фактором, который порождает подобные
взаимосвязи"; on Deutsch: "Опять полез антропоцентризм", "Здравый смысл вернулся, ура!"); article 03 ("Когда цветы
сворачиваются перед дождем... прогнозируя наступление тех или иных событий в будущем"); article 08 (Hegel's law as
"фактор существования времени как такового"). Invented for the frame (Oleg's side): the dream, the cup, the soup
and the chicken, the ladle and the commission, the knee, the greenhouse loan; on Andrey's side, the deck in the
jacket. Not used: "the parabola simply is" (the plan's material; the printed book and the frames carry the same
point), Eddington's "arrow of time" (the word "arrow" is not used at all), Maxwell's demon, Hawking's three arrows,
Popper's pond (the image is used without the name), the Andromeda paradox (17), Petrov and Arkhipov, weather
models after GraphCast, any figure for the Landauer bound, the names of all people and companies.

**Figures**: `one-order-and-all-the-rest` (a logarithmic scale: one way "by suits", the seconds since the beginning
of the world, the orders of thirty-six cards), `traces-lie-on-one-side` (seven pages from order to shuffled, solid
arrows of traces into "now" from one side, dashed arrows of a forecast to the other), `what-a-forecast-is-made-of`
(the table: the flower, Oleg and the chicken, the sunrise, the weather network, the bank's program, a prophetic
dream).

**Left for later**: whether anybody understands what such a machine does, and a proof that one machine made and
another checked → 60 (Oleg's last doubt: "With weather, tomorrow does come... Who checked it? — Another machine. —
And who understood it?"; 59 says only that the weather network is checked against tomorrow and that nobody
understands it "in the sense you mean"); the map with the map-maker on it, that is, the forecaster who "is himself
in the book" → 63 (one sentence here); the weapon of 59 turned on Andrey's own forecasts ("a memory of tomorrow...
can be wrong") → 66; why the deck lay by suits stays open, with Oleg's half tick; Hegel's law stays a sentence;
the rest of the promised series "outside of time" (Parmenides, the reflection equations) is not touched; a row
"memory and forecast" for the portrait (more traces, a better rule, the same ceiling, a letter without a date) →
64; things to watch, not marks: Oleg's notebook of dreams (the wager of 39, now also the stated refutation of 59);
the outcome of Oleg's court is still free for a later opening.

## 60. Science with No One to Understand It

**Established**
- The answer to the teaser of 59 ("a machine has proved a theorem... Who checked it? — Another machine. — And who
  understood it?"). Oleg comes with a printed sheet folded in four; the chicken of 59 was "roast, with garlic. As
  remembered. Only she put potatoes round it, and those I had not remembered" ("Roughly, then"). **The news is real
  and dated by the day of writing**: on 20 November 2026 a mathematician at a maker of talking machines put out a
  proof of the Riemann hypothesis as about 17 million lines for a checking program (Lean; no names in the dialogue);
  on 21 November the keepers of the program wrote "We have full confidence that the proof is correct"; a
  mathematician of the same week: exceedingly likely to be correct, and "no human understands it yet". Oleg's
  position: "a proof is when I am shown, and I see. If nobody saw, it is not a proof. It is a rumour with a stamp".
- **The rule of the cycle is kept by a detour**: Oleg names the machines at the start, and Andrey puts the sheet
  "under the stone for an hour" ("You named them, not I"); the sheet comes back past the middle.
- **To understand, by Socratic descent.** The current in a wire: "like water in a pipe", so "like light"; in fact
  the electrons crawl a fraction of a millimetre a second, and in an alternating current "shake on the spot" (the
  pipe full of peas). "When the teacher said 'like water in a pipe', what did you feel? — 'Ah.' It became clear. —
  And for fifty years it was clear. With a wrong answer inside." **The "ah" is "a bell. It rings when a new thing
  lies down on an old one"**: the author's line of 15 (understanding is the perception of an image, the signal
  "image recognised") and the cashier of 36 and 41; "she does not check what she pays for".
- **The middle world** (the plan's Dawkins, unnamed: "a biologist"): the old images come from the stick of 58;
  from a hair to the horizon is about eight noughts, and science reckons along about forty-six. The electron of 56
  is neither picture; Feynman's sentence of 1964 (unnamed) and the electron's magnet measured in 2023 to the
  twelfth figure: **"With no bell. — For a hundred years."** **"Two different things, then. The check, and the
  'ah'. They used to come in one envelope, and we took them for one."**
- **Mathematics, where "there is no tomorrow"**: a proof is the "ah" step by step, one thought at a time (03);
  "and if there are more steps than a life has days?" **The four pencils** (the great-nephew's contour maps): asked
  in 1852, answered in 1976 with nearly two thousand cases and about 1,200 hours of machine time; "applauded
  politely"; Thurston (unnamed) on what the quarrel was about; in 1996 four others gave up checking it and made
  their own with 633 cases, again for a machine; "Why four and not five? — Because in six hundred and thirty-three
  cases it came out so. — That is the whole 'why' there is. Fifty years this year."
- **The oranges** (Kepler, 1611; Hales, 1998, unnamed): 250 pages and three gigabytes, a dozen referees, four
  years, "ninety-nine per cent certain", and the editor's letter that they "had run out of energy"; Deligne's "I
  believe in a proof if I understand it" (unnamed) is Oleg's sentence. The author then rewrote his own proof for a
  checking program, 2003–2014: "the question was closed. Not by the twelve."
- **The template** (Oleg's of 1984, from 57; he says the word himself): the inspector "had no idea how the casting
  was poured... He needed to know the template. And the drawing." The accepting part of a checker is a few hundred
  lines (2014) to a few thousand (2026): **"Understanding has not gone anywhere: it has moved from the casting to
  the template."** Said honestly: a template can be filed wrong (the same man found a hole in 2003; several holes
  in the summer of 2026, through one of which went "a short false proof... of his own theorem about the oranges";
  mended; found by machines set to look); several templates by different hands; two carry a proof that they pass
  nothing false, "checked, yes, by a template. That is a circle" → 63. **The drawing is the weak place and a man
  reads it** (Fermat's theorem written with nought let in: "The program does not blush").
- **The sheet again.** Printed, the 17 million lines pass 250,000 pages (the author's own footnote); Oleg's
  arithmetic: ten pages a day, sixty-eight years. The first theorem begins with "if a number is not equal to one
  half, then it is either greater or less". One checker took three and a quarter minutes, the most careful three
  days. **The second half of that Friday**: a 940-page proof by a man, "all human", with possible problems reported
  within hours: "of the two, the one written to be understood is doubted. And the one nobody understands is
  believed. — For the first of them people had to be the template." The man who led the first "is presenting the
  world with a complete argument before having mastered it". Fermat's theorem written out for the template in
  September 2026 (13 million lines, eleven days): "it checks out", and "mathematically this tells us essentially
  nothing". A mathematician thanked in the announcement: the object of mathematics is the question "why?", "and
  this does not answer it at all" — "And he is right. — About what mathematicians want."
- **The exposure: "understandable" is a property of a pair.** The proof of 2012 (Mochizuki, unnamed): by a man, for
  men, not agreed on in fourteen years; the July 2026 report of the group that tried to write it for the template.
  "Computable by whom? Knowable by whom?" (15); the "open" of 59; "The weed. Again."
- **The measuring-system rule of 03 applied to explanations** (the plan's): Gaika's injection, "a thunderstorm in
  the shape of the master"; "'Walk' I can explain. 'Encephalitis', no"; **"an explanation from a mind above yours
  either lies down on your images, with losses... or does not lie down at all. Then it is not an explanation for
  you. It is a phenomenon of nature."** The 17 million lines are being studied "the way a geologist studies a rock...
  the rule of the first autumn, come true in one week".
- **Oleg's first strong objection**: five days later the proof was 300,000 and then 76,000 lines; "They will chew
  it, and in a year there will be a textbook." Conceded as "your best card" ("not a life, a hard year"). **Shortness
  is partly a task and partly our way** (46): we squeeze because a long thing does not go into the head or through
  the straw of 45; Oleg corrects himself with 59 ("a short rule works outside the yard... My dream book is long.
  The law of the lever is short"); "To squeeze is a task for any mind... To squeeze down to our head is our way.
  The heir will squeeze too. To its own pot" (53); the compressed signal of 29; "Short for whom?"
- **Outside mathematics**: the weather network of 59 has been looked into (features for cyclones, the daily round,
  sea ice; "largely unknown"); the proteins of 16 (over 200 million shapes against about 190 thousand from
  experiment; "it predicts the fold and not the folding").
- **The debt of 39 is paid: three examiners.** The template (only where there is a drawing); tomorrow ("the stick,
  and it moves at the speed of the world"); another machine: "with the third we are the ant... The first two
  examiners are still in our hands. That is what I would watch, and not who understood."
- **Against himself**: the chess notions taken out of a self-taught program and taught to four world champions
  (2025); "look at the sieve": only what could be taught passed, and the authors call it a proof of concept.
- **The split** (the plan's piece): "Which half does the bridge pay for? — The first. — Which did the
  mathematicians love? — The second... To predict, a man had to understand: he had no other way. Now there is one."
  Understanding stays "as music. As chess after 1997"; the university rule of November 2026 (no weight to written
  work without evidence that the authors understand it) is a receipt of 41: "They are guarding the trade. — They
  are guarding what they love. I do not laugh at it."
- **The debt of 53 and 57 (the shares in what a pair has found)**, counted as in 12 by five questions: who asked
  (Riemann), who chose and led (a man), who found the road at the finish ("by his own words, that one"), who
  checked (the templates; people read the drawing), who understood (nobody yet). "Under the four pencils of 1976
  the last line was empty too, so the empty line is not the news... Under your sheet the line 'who found' has
  changed hands."
- **Oleg's strongest objection is the plan's (Deutsch, unnamed; his second book, retold by the great-nephew; the
  author read the first with a reader in 2013)**: man is over a threshold, a universal explainer; paper and time;
  "there is only 'not yet'". **Conceded in principle** ("I shall not break it"). The answer is the two
  computabilities of 15 ("the one in principle, and the one in time for supper"), the straw and the common part of
  53, and: "who is the universal explainer there: the man, or the pair?" (Oleg: the pair; but "with the screen
  down, afterwards, the man can tell it" — "Then he has, and it is his"). **"I do not say there is a wall. I say
  there is a price, and a queue"**: 722 machine-written papers in one month. "Your physicist is right about every
  single proof. I am speaking of the stream."
- **The planned mark is declared late**: "that by the autumn of 2031 science will accept a result whose derivation
  no man has read through. I am late. It happened last Saturday."
- **The mark of 30 (a Millennium Prize Problem solved mainly by a machine by the autumn of 2036, one in two) is
  named and NOT ticked**: the prize's rules (publication, two years, general acceptance) and "mainly by a machine"
  ("a man led it") are still to be weighed; "it goes into the list as it is". Said now: "I gave it ten years and
  even odds. If it stands, it took a few months, and I was not bold. I was timid again."
- **Two new marks for the autumn of 2031.** (1) Andrey's: at least one of the old journals of the first rank will
  be printing theorems whose proofs its referees did not read (they read the statement, the checker reads the
  rest), three to one. (2) "For your physicist, and I put it against myself": the proof of November 2026 will have
  an account written for people of which those in the field say "we understand why", two to one that it will. "If
  both come true... we are both right, and that is the likeliest. One proof gets chewed. The queue grows."
- Nobody says the forbidden word; twenty-eighth tick "again": "Came with a sheet, to find out who understood. Came
  out at this: understanding is what is left to us of science. As a hobby."
- Strengths stated: solid (the four pencils and the oranges; the twelfth figure; the news, "read in the statement
  of the checkers and on the page where the checks are listed"; the holes in the template); told by a newspaper
  and not read in the original (the problems in the human proof); argued about (how much can be got out of a
  network and taught); the author's (understanding as the perception of an image; the rule of the ant); today's
  (the template in place of the "ah"; the three examiners; shortness as partly a task and partly our way, "the
  last I cannot divide cleanly"). **What would refute it**: if without "why" the calculating stops ("those who do
  not understand begin to be wrong where those who understand were not"), or if the queue is chewed as fast as it
  grows.
- The lock with 28 and 30, in the closing lines: "nobody is taking your 'ah' away... It is the one thing in this
  story that nobody else needs. — So what have we been doing here, sixty evenings? — Understanding. — Production?
  — None."
- **A declared interest is written into the footer** (both languages): the proof of the Riemann hypothesis and the
  formal proof of Fermat's theorem were made with models of Anthropic, the maker of the model that wrote the
  evening; the news is told from the checkers' statement and the pages of those who repeated the checks. No company
  or person is named in the dialogue.

**Images and devices used**: the twenty-first entry in the notebook of dreams (the pay office: a sealed envelope,
"Counted. Do not open. Sign"; nobody rang), which returns once ("in one envelope"); the letter box, "Empty"; the
sheet folded in four, under the stone for an hour, and Oleg's sum on its back; water in a pipe and the pipe full of
peas; the bell and the cashier; puddles, the tap, the pump in the yard; from a hair to the horizon; "eight out of
forty-six"; the twelfth figure; four pencils and the contour map; oranges in a pyramid; "one per cent left over";
the template and the drawing; "they sent him his own casting. Through a hole in the template"; "the program does
not blush"; sixty-eight years with no Sundays; "to understand his own proof"; Gaika's injection, treachery and
weather; "walk" and "encephalitis"; the geologist and the rock; the dream book and the law of the lever; "short
for whom?" ("everything of yours ends in 'for whom'"); three examiners; the sieve; the bridge that pays; the
receipt; five questions and the line that changed hands; tape longer than a life; "a price, and a queue"; the
teaser (the flag, "somebody has to stand on the spot", the reddish star: "Is anybody there now? — Yes. — People?
— No people").

**Facts used**. **Read in the source on the day of writing** (28 November 2026; fetched pages, text extracted):
the joint statement of the Lean FRO and the Mathlib Initiative, 21 November 2026 (17 million lines; "full
confidence"; the four criteria; checkers "implemented in different programming languages", two of which "come with
a formal proof... that they cannot accept a false proof"); A. Sutherland's page of verification files (175,187
files, 17,374,215 lines; con-leche 3 min 15 s on 64 threads; Lean's default kernel 48 h and its paranoid kernel
72 h 20 min on one thread); L. Alpöge's four-page remark (working with Claude; "Claude far outpaced me near the
finish line"; "presenting the world with a complete argument before having mastered"; the print-out that "hit
250,000+ pages"; Theorem 1 and the totally ordered reals; "it will be massively simplified"; the people thanked);
the README of the shorter version (about 300,000 and about 76,000 lines; "passed machine checks only... No
mathematician has read it through"); T. Tao's post of 20 November 2026 ("an (enormous) Lean-only proof"; the
Navier–Stokes results of the same day are not used); Quanta's updates of 20 and 24 November 2026 (the 940-page
manuscript "all human, with no AI", uploaded the same day by the president of the AMS; number theorists
"withholding judgement"; Radziwill's "the question 'why?'"; the Johns Hopkins criteria; mathematicians
reverse-engineering the Lean code with AI); The Conversation, 23 November 2026 (Litt's two points; "potential
problems had been found within hours": secondary); K. Buzzard's posts of 4 September 2026 (Fermat's Last Theorem,
over 13.4 million lines in 11 days, "it checks out", "mathematically this work... tells us essentially nothing")
and of 12 November 2026 (the mis-stated Fermat with ℕ containing 0; 722 papers in one dump, about 45% with Lean,
three of the others retracted for a sign error); T. Hales's guest post of 9 October 2026 (the HOL Light bug of
2003, a kernel of "a few hundred lines"; the Lean kernel "several thousand lines"; the "Summer of Soundness Bugs"
and the "short illicit proof of the Kepler conjecture"; "detected by frontier model AI in the hands of security
researchers"); the Clay Institute's rules (qualifying outlet, two years, general acceptance); Schut et al., *PNAS*
2025 (abstract, through PMC: teachability and novelty filters, four world champions, "proof of concept");
MacMillan & Ouellette, arXiv 2512.24440 (abstract); Chen et al., "Protein folds vs. protein folding", *PNAS* 2022
(through PMC: "this is not folding prediction"; "we know how it ends, but we don't know why"); the abstract of
arXiv 2605.20695 (not used). **By a research pass, with its own marks**: Appel and Haken (about 1,200 hours; 1,936
configurations in the 1976 preprints and 1,482 in print, hence "nearly two thousand"; the polite applause, after
R. Wilson); Robertson, Sanders, Seymour & Thomas (633 configurations; "tried to verify the Appel–Haken proof, but
soon gave up"); Thurston, *Bull. AMS* 30 (1994), read in the PDF; Szpiro, *Nature* 424, 12 (2003), read directly
(250 pages, three gigabytes, a dozen referees, four years; "99% certain" is Szpiro's paraphrase of Fejes Tóth's
report of July 2002, not a quotation; the editor's "run out of energy"; Deligne's sentence); Hales et al., *Forum of
Mathematics, Pi* 5 (2017), 22 authors, Flyspeck January 2003 – August 2014; Mochizuki (August 2012; PRIMS 57
(2021), about 720 pages; Scholze and Stix, 2018) and the interim report of Project LANA, July 2026 ("did not
conclude that IUT theory is wrong"; "a point where a proof is not fully written"; no complete consensus);
Feynman, Messenger Lectures 1964, lecture 6; Fan et al., *PRL* 130 (2023), 0.13 parts per trillion; DeepMind's
post of 28 July 2022 (over 200 million structures; about 190,000 in the Protein Data Bank). **From the writer's
memory, not re-read**: the drift speed of electrons in a household wire (a fraction of a millimetre a second);
Guthrie and the counties of England, 1852; the sizes on the scale (a collider at about 10⁻¹⁹ m; the visible world
about 9·10²⁶ m across; the horizon at about 5 km); Deutsch's "universal explainer" (*The Beginning of Infinity*,
2011; the research pass found the claim only in an excerpt, so it is retold and not quoted); Dawkins's "Middle
World" (TED, 2005; the link was checked, the wording was not); that the statement of the Riemann hypothesis in the
library is short. **Computed**: 250,000 pages at ten a day ≈ 68.5 years; 76,000 lines ≈ 1,100 pages at the ratio
of the full version (about 70 lines a page), "by my own rough measure". The author's sources: article 15
("Достижение понимания — это просто восприятие образа"; сигнал «образ распознан»; "Пощупать математические истины
непосредственно, «мимо образов» мы не можем"; "вычислимо КЕМ?"); article 03 (the measuring-system rule: "поведение
со степенью логичности, выходящей за пределы собственной логичности человека, будет оценено человеком как просто
нелогичное"); the 2013 reading of Deutsch's *The Fabric of Reality* on the forum. Invented for the frame (Oleg's
side): the dream, the sheet, the potatoes, the great-nephew's contour maps and his retelling of the book, Gaika's
injection. Not used: Haldane's "queerer than we can suppose", Tymoczko (1979), Halmos, the daughters who checked
the microfiche, Gonthier's Coq proof by name, the classification of finite simple groups and its formal proof
announced in 2026, the Erdős problems and the unit-distance disproof of May 2026 (it was digested by people within
days: a case for Oleg's side that the evening does not need twice), the Navier–Stokes claims of 2026, the IMO
results, the Leiden Declaration and Hexagon, the names of all people and companies.

**Figures**: `eight-noughts-of-forty-six` (the scale of sizes with the strip from a hair to the horizon),
`time-to-read-and-time-to-check` (a logarithmic scale of time: two checkers, the twelve referees, the rewriting of
the oranges, the conjecture of 2012, one reader), `who-asked-found-checked-understood` (the table of five proofs).

**Left for later**: why only machines are on Mars, and whether "somebody has to stand on the spot" → 61 (Oleg's
last doubt: "Calculating is theirs. But going there, seeing with one's own eyes, sticking in a flag: that is
ours"; the teaser ends on the plan's epigraph); who checks the checker, the template checked by a template → 63
("That is a circle, and it has an evening of its own: the map with the map on it"; 60 establishes the certificate
that the plan's 63 lets Oleg use, and says where it exists: only where there is a drawing; that no template exists
for wants is not said here); a row "knowledge" for the portrait (squeezes to its own pot; examined by the
template, by tomorrow and by its own kind; an explanation that is weather to us) → 64; the two marks of 60 for the
autumn of 2031, to be checked with the list of 30 and the marks of 53 → 67; **the mark of 30 on a Millennium
Prize Problem must be weighed at the check** (the proof of 20 November 2026: the prize's rules, and how much of
it was the man's); things to watch, not marks: which of the three examiners is at work, and how long the queue of
checked and unread results is; whether the 940-page proof stands is not followed; the outcome of Oleg's court is
still free for a later opening.

## 61. Why Only Robots Live on Mars

**Established**
- The answer to the teaser of 60 ("going there, seeing with one's own eyes, sticking in a flag: that is ours...
  Is anybody there now? — Yes. — People? — No people"). Oleg comes late, when Mars is over the firs. **The
  population of Mars on the day of writing (28 November 2026)**: two on the ground (one driving since August 2012,
  one since February 2021, "with a sore throat": its main radio failed a month ago), six in orbit "if all of them
  still answer", one written off in the summer. "Eight... Not one of them breathes." No craft is named in the
  dialogue.
- **The rule of the cycle**: the rovers are the topic from the first line, as "equipment"; Oleg's "Our eyes, carried
  out on a long pole. People are there. Through them" is written into the notebook and "the line left open" until
  past the middle. The talking machines are named by Oleg ("Don't tell me. One of those"); the heir in orbit comes
  in through Oleg's own news ("A million satellites... For those ones").
- **The count of distances.** The four who flew round the Moon in April 2026 reached 406 thousand km, "a second and
  a third, by light": the furthest a human being has been; twenty-eight people in history have been that far,
  twelve walked, the last left in December 1972 (Oleg "was in the second form"). The craft of 1977 reached one
  light-day on 18 November 2026; Oleg's division: "sixty-four thousand times further". **Said honestly**: it crossed
  the mark ill (only the bare tone of the transmitter since the fourteenth); "It may be that this was its last
  mark."
- **The descent: the fishing list.** Oleg's list for three days (food, water, a jacket, matches, petrol for the way
  back) has no air on it: "It is everywhere. — Everywhere within some five kilometres upward." The same list for two
  and a half years, counted "as supply counts": a kilogram of oxygen and about four litres of water a day (98% turned
  round, "and the plant that turns it round is carried too"); the dose there and back, two thirds of a sievert
  against six tenths allowed for a career ("The road alone is more than a life's allowance"); bones; the ticket back
  and "the petrol for carrying that petrol"; the plan of 2008: 825–1,250 tonnes in orbit for six people, "over a
  hundred tonnes a head". For the machine: none of it; a lump of plutonium, 110 watts; "Nobody bought it one. And
  nobody called that cruelty"; built for ninety days, worked 5,111; told to sleep for a month.
- **The task and the way (46) applied**: the task is "to get there. To look. To poke it with the stick and send back
  what came out"; our way is "to carry the lake along". **"A man does not go into space. A small piece of the Earth
  goes, with a man inside. A spacesuit is the Earth cut to his size."** The strip of conditions as the sister of the
  middle world of 60.
- **The head start** (the plan's piece): on the Earth nearly everything is there for us for nothing; the staircase
  and the door handle of 16; "here the machine plays away from home, on a field built for us and by us. Where does
  it not? — Where both arrive as strangers." "One place of that kind is known. And the score there is on your page."
- **Oleg's first strong objection** (Squyres, unnamed: "the man who was in charge of two earlier rovers"): what a
  rover does in a perfect day a man would do in less than a minute (2005); "a good week" (2009); the geologist's
  count (Crawford, unnamed): two men drove nearly 36 km in three days of December 1972, a rover needed eight years.
  Conceded ("the strongest thing that can be said for the flag, and about that rover it is true"). The answer: one
  side of the scales is nailed down (the geologist of 2026 is no quicker than that of 1972) and the other moves (the
  rovers of 2004 found their own way over about a twentieth of the road, the present one over more than nine tenths;
  it passed the old record of 45 km in October 2026, in under six years against fourteen); and the price: "half a
  trillion" (an engineer's estimate of 2016, "the agency has no price of its own") against 2.7 billion, "a hundred
  and eighty of them for one expedition". "He was measuring by the day on the spot. Supply measures by the tonne and
  by the rouble, and it is supply that signs."
- **"Our eyes" weeded**: the symmetry test of 02; the author's forum post of 2016 (the rover must be given a want
  and find the moves itself; "more than twenty minutes one way") **corrected openly**: "He named the far end; it is
  from four to twenty-four". The two drives of December 2025 whose route was drawn by a talking machine (456 m). The
  five questions of 60 asked again: who wanted (people), who took the pictures (a machine overhead), who drew the
  road ("one of those"), who drove (the rover), who checked (a program on a model, and people). Oleg: "So we are the
  wants. And the signature" (24). **"'Our eyes' is the column 'who', filled in with ourselves"**, said the long way
  (50); the great-nephew's phone held up to the waves: "Were you at the sea? — No. I saw the sea. — We have seen
  Mars."
